\textnormal{{f}}_{m+1}(\textnormal{{g}}(\delta),\textnormal{{g}}_{n+1}(\delta,\underline{x}))\equiv f(\textnormal{{g}}(\delta),g(\textnormal{{g}}(\delta),\textnormal{{q}}(\delta)^{*}\underline{x}))\overset{q(\textnormal{{g}}(\delta),\textnormal{{q}}(\delta)^{*}\underline{x})}{=}\textnormal{{q}}(\delta)^{*}\underline{x}

in context
\delta:\Delta,\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta)
. Moreover:

\displaystyle\textnormal{{g}}_{n+1}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma),\textnormal{{f}}_{m+1}(\gamma,x))

\displaystyle\equiv g(\textnormal{{g}}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma)),\textnormal{{q}}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))^{*}\textnormal{{f}}_{m+1}(\gamma,x))

\displaystyle=g(\textnormal{{g}}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma)),\textnormal{{f}}(\textnormal{{p}}(\gamma))^{*}\textnormal{{f}}_{m+1}(\gamma,x))

\displaystyle=\textnormal{{p}}(\gamma)^{*}x

in context
\gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma)
, where the first identity type is inhabited as one can assume w.l.o.g. that:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{q}}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))=\textnormal{{f}}(\textnormal{{p}}(\gamma))

and the second by applying based (generalised) path induction
n
times on
\textnormal{{p}}_{1}(\gamma)
,
\textnormal{{p}}_{2}(\gamma)
, …,
\textnormal{{p}}_{n}(\gamma)
and finally using that
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;p(\gamma,x):g(\gamma,f(\gamma,x))=x
.

In conclusion, we saw that we can augment the contexts
\gamma:\Gamma
and
\delta:\Delta
in:

\gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma)\textnormal{ and }\delta:\Delta,\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta)

respectively, in such a way that they continue being homotopy equivalent. In fact the extended context morphisms:

\succ

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma):\Delta,\textnormal{{f}}_{m+1}(\gamma,x):A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))
i.e.

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;(\textnormal{{f}},\textnormal{{f}}_{m+1})(\gamma,x):\Delta,A^{\prime}

\succ

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta:\Delta,\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{g}}(\delta):\Gamma,\textnormal{{g}}_{n+1}(\delta,\underline{x}):A(\textnormal{{g}}(\delta))
i.e.

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta:\Delta,\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;(\textnormal{{g}},\textnormal{{g}}_{n+1})(\delta,\underline{x}):\Gamma,A

constitute a homotopy equivalence, since:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{p}}(\gamma):\textnormal{{g}}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))=\gamma
and
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{g}}_{n+1}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma),\textnormal{{f}}_{m+1}(\gamma,x))=\textnormal{{p}}(\gamma)^{*}x

that is:

\displaystyle{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{g}}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma)),\textnormal{{g}}_{n+1}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma),\textnormal{{f}}_{m+1}(\gamma,x))

\displaystyle=\gamma,x\textnormal{ \, i.e.}

\displaystyle{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;(\textnormal{{g}},\textnormal{{g}}_{n+1})((\textnormal{{f}},\textnormal{{f}}_{m+1})(\gamma,x))

\displaystyle=\gamma,x

and since:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta:\Delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{q}}(\delta):\textnormal{{f}}(\textnormal{{g}}(\delta))=\delta
and
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta:\Delta,\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{f}}_{m+1}(\textnormal{{g}}(\delta),\textnormal{{g}}_{n+1}(\delta,\underline{x}))=\textnormal{{q}}(\delta)^{*}\underline{x}

that is:

\displaystyle{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta:\Delta,\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{f}}(\textnormal{{g}}(\delta)),\textnormal{{f}}_{m+1}(\textnormal{{g}}(\delta),\textnormal{{g}}_{n+1}(\delta,\underline{x}))

\displaystyle=\delta,\underline{x}\textnormal{ \, i.e.}

\displaystyle{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta:\Delta,\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;(\textnormal{{f}},\textnormal{{f}}_{m+1})((\textnormal{{g}},\textnormal{{g}}_{n+1})(\delta,\underline{x}))

\displaystyle=\delta,\underline{x}.

Let us summarise this into the following:

Lemma 3.1.1

(Extension)
.

Let
\gamma:\Gamma
and
\delta:\Delta
and let:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma):\Delta

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta:\Delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{g}}(\delta):\Gamma

be a context homotopy equivalence
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
. If we are given types:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}\text{ and }{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta:\Delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A^{\prime}(\delta):\textsc{Type}

together with a homotopy equivalence:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\gamma,x):A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}:A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}):A(\gamma)

between
A(\gamma)
and
A^{\prime}(\delta)
relative to
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
then:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;(\textnormal{{f}},\textnormal{{f}}_{m+1})(\gamma,x):\Delta,A^{\prime}

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta:\Delta,\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;(\textnormal{{g}},\textnormal{{g}}_{n+1})(\delta,\underline{x}):\Gamma,A

is a context homotopy equivalence
(\textnormal{{f}},\textnormal{{f}}_{m+1};\textnormal{{g}},\textnormal{{g}}_{n+1})
, where:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{f}}_{m+1}(\gamma,x)\equiv f(\gamma,x):A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta:\Delta,\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{g}}_{n+1}(\delta,\underline{x})\equiv g(\textnormal{{g}}(\delta),\textnormal{{q}}(\delta)^{*}\underline{x}):A(\textnormal{{g}}(\delta))
.

We call
(\textnormal{{f}},\textnormal{{f}}_{m+1};\textnormal{{g}},\textnormal{{g}}_{n+1})
the
extension of
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
via
(f;g)
.

In the next subsections we analyse specific shapes of homotopy equivalences between types relative to a given context homotopy equivalence. The first regards the ones coming from an application of the dependent product constructor.

3.2
Dependent product of homotopy equivalences

We start the current subsection by briefly describing the following data:

Data.
Let us assume that
\gamma:\Gamma
and
\delta:\Delta
are contexts and that
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
is a context homotopy equivalence between them. Moreover, let us assume that we are given judgements:

\succ

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}
and
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;B(\gamma,x):\textsc{Type}

\succ

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta:\Delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A^{\prime}(\delta):\textsc{Type}
and
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta:\Delta,\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;B^{\prime}(\delta,\underline{x}):\textsc{Type}

together with a homotopy equivalence
(f_{1};g_{1})
:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f_{1}(\gamma,x):A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}:A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g_{1}(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}):A(\gamma)

between
A(\gamma)
and
A^{\prime}(\delta)
relative to
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
, and a homotopy equivalence
(f_{2};g_{2})
:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma),y:B(\gamma,x)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f_{2}(\gamma,x,y):B^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma),f_{1}(\gamma,x))

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma),\underline{y}^{\prime}:B^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma),f_{1}(\gamma,x))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g_{2}(\gamma,x,\underline{y}^{\prime}):B(\gamma,x)

between
B(\gamma,x)
and
B^{\prime}(\delta,\underline{x})
relative to the extension
(\textnormal{{f}},\textnormal{{f}}_{m+1};\textnormal{{g}},\textnormal{{g}}_{n+1})
of
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
via
(f_{1};g_{1})
(see
Lemma

3.1.1
).

Fact.
We can use these data to construct a homotopy equivalence
(f^{\Pi};g^{\Pi})
between the types
\Pi_{x:A(\gamma)}B(\gamma,x)
and
\Pi_{\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta)}B^{\prime}(\delta,\underline{x})
relative to
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
as follows, leading to Lemma
3.2.1
below. The reader may go through the proof under the additional assumption that the contexts
\gamma
and
\delta
are empty. However, here we present the proof in full generality because there are several (homotopic) ways to define the pair
(f^{\Pi};g^{\Pi})
and the one we choose here will determine the style of the subsequent proofs. A similar remark applies to
Sections

3.3
and
3.4
.

Let us start by considering homotopies:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;p_{1}(\gamma,x):x=g_{1}(\gamma,f_{1}(\gamma,x))

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}:A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;q_{1}(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}):\underline{x}^{\prime}=f_{1}(\gamma,g_{1}(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}))

and homotopies:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma),y:B(\gamma,x)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;p_{2}(\gamma,x,y):y=g_{2}(\gamma,x,f_{2}(\gamma,x,y))

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma),\underline{y}^{\prime}:B^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma),f_{1}(\gamma,x))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;q_{2}(\gamma,x,\underline{y}^{\prime}):\underline{y}^{\prime}=f_{2}(\gamma,x,g_{2}(\gamma,x,\underline{y}^{\prime}))

as in our assumptions.

Let us fix the context
\gamma:\Gamma,z:\Pi_{x:A(\gamma)}B(\gamma,x)
and let us observe that
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,z,x:A\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{ev}}(z,x):B(\gamma,x)
, hence:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,z,x\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f_{2}(\gamma,x,\textnormal{{ev}}(z,x)):B^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma),f_{1}(\gamma,x)).

Since
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}:A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g_{1}(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}):A(\gamma)
, then:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,z,\underline{x}^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f_{2}(\gamma,g_{1}(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}),\textnormal{{ev}}(z,g_{1}(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}))):B^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma),f_{1}(\gamma,g_{1}(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime})))

hence:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,z,\underline{x}^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;q_{1}(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime})^{*}f_{2}(\gamma,g_{1}(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}),\textnormal{{ev}}(z,g_{1}(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}))):B^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma),\underline{x}^{\prime}).

We conclude that:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,z\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f^{\Pi}(\gamma,z)\equiv\lambda\underline{x}^{\prime}:A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))\;.\;q_{1}(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime})^{*}f_{2}(\gamma,g_{1}(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}),\textnormal{{ev}}(z,g_{1}(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime})))

is a term of type:

\Pi_{\underline{x}^{\prime}:A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))}B(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma),\underline{x}^{\prime})\equiv[\Pi_{\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta)}B^{\prime}(\delta,\underline{x})](\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma)).

Vice versa, let us fix the context
\gamma:\Gamma,\underline{z}^{\prime}:\Pi_{\underline{x}^{\prime}:A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))}B(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma),\underline{x}^{\prime})
and let us observe that:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,\underline{z}^{\prime},\underline{x}^{\prime}:A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{ev}}(\underline{z}^{\prime},\underline{x}^{\prime}):B^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma),\underline{x}^{\prime})

hence
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,\underline{z}^{\prime},x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{ev}}(\underline{z}^{\prime},f_{1}(\gamma,x)):B^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma),f_{1}(\gamma,x))
and therefore:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,\underline{z}^{\prime},x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g_{2}(\gamma,x,\textnormal{{ev}}(\underline{z}^{\prime},f_{1}(\gamma,x))):B(\gamma,x).

We conclude that:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,\underline{z}^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g^{\Pi}(\gamma,\underline{z}^{\prime})\equiv\lambda x:A(\gamma)\;.\;g_{2}(\gamma,x,\textnormal{{ev}}(\underline{z}^{\prime},f_{1}(\gamma,x))):\Pi_{x:A(\gamma)}B(\gamma,x).

We claim that
(f^{\Pi};g^{\Pi})
is a homotopy equivalence between
\Pi_{x:A(\gamma)}B(\gamma,x)
and
\Pi_{\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta)}B^{\prime}(\delta,\underline{x})
relative to
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
. We start by verifying that
g^{\Pi}(\gamma,f^{\Pi}(\gamma,z))\equiv z
in context
\gamma,z
. Let
\underline{z}^{\prime}\equiv f^{\Pi}(\gamma,z)
and let us observe that:

\displaystyle\textnormal{{ev}}(\underline{z}^{\prime},f_{1}(\gamma,x))

\displaystyle=q_{1}(\gamma,f_{1}(\gamma,x))^{*}f_{2}(\gamma,g_{1}(\gamma,f_{1}(\gamma,x)),\textnormal{{ev}}(z,g_{1}(\gamma,f_{1}(\gamma,x))))

\displaystyle=f_{1}(\gamma,p_{1}(\gamma,x))^{*}f_{2}(\gamma,g_{1}(\gamma,f_{1}(\gamma,x)),\textnormal{{ev}}(z,g_{1}(\gamma,f_{1}(\gamma,x))))

\displaystyle=f_{2}(\gamma,x,\textnormal{{ev}}(z,x))

where the first equality follows by
\beta
-reduction, the second because
q_{1}(\gamma,f_{1}(\gamma,x))=f_{1}(\gamma,p_{1}(\gamma,x))
without loss of generality and the third by based (generalised) path induction on
p_{1}(\gamma,x)
. By propositional functoriality:

g_{2}(\gamma,x,\textnormal{{ev}}(\underline{z}^{\prime},f_{1}(\gamma,x)))=g_{2}(\gamma,x,f_{2}(\gamma,x,\textnormal{{ev}}(z,x)))\overset{p_{2}(\gamma,x,\textnormal{{ev}}(z,x))}{=}\textnormal{{ev}}(z,x)

hence by propositional
\Pi
-extensionality:

g^{\Pi}(\gamma,f^{\Pi}(\gamma,z))\equiv g^{\Pi}(\gamma,\underline{z}^{\prime})=\lambda x:A(\gamma)\;.\;\textnormal{{ev}}(z,x)=z

where the last identity type is inhabited by
\eta
-expansion.

Vice versa, let us verify that
f^{\Pi}(\gamma,z)=\underline{z}^{\prime}
, where
z\equiv g^{\Pi}(\gamma,\underline{z}^{\prime})
. At first, we observe that:

\textnormal{{ev}}(z,g_{1}(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}))=g_{2}(\gamma,g_{1}(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}),\textnormal{{ev}}(\underline{z}^{\prime},f_{1}(\gamma,g_{1}(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}))))

hence:

\displaystyle f_{2}(\gamma,g_{1}(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}),\textnormal{{ev}}(z,g_{1}(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime})))

\displaystyle=f_{2}(\gamma,g_{1}(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}),g_{2}(\gamma,g_{1}(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}),\textnormal{{ev}}(\underline{z}^{\prime},f_{1}(\gamma,g_{1}(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime})))))

\displaystyle=\textnormal{{ev}}(\underline{z}^{\prime},f_{1}(\gamma,g_{1}(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime})))

where the first identity type is inhabited by propositional functoriality and the second one by the term:

q_{2}(\gamma,g_{1}(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}),\textnormal{{ev}}(\underline{z}^{\prime},f_{1}(\gamma,g_{1}(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime})))).

Secondly, we observe that:

q_{1}(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime})^{*}f_{2}(\gamma,g_{1}(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}),\textnormal{{ev}}(z,g_{1}(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime})))=q_{1}(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime})^{*}\textnormal{{ev}}(\underline{z}^{\prime},f_{1}(\gamma,g_{1}(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime})))=\textnormal{{ev}}(\underline{z}^{\prime},\underline{x}^{\prime})

where the former equality follows by propositional functoriality and the latter by based (generalised) path induction on
q_{1}(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime})
. Therefore:

f^{\Pi}(\gamma,g^{\Pi}(\gamma,\underline{z}^{\prime}))\equiv f^{\Pi}(\gamma,z)=\lambda\underline{x}^{\prime}:A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))\;.\;\textnormal{{ev}}(\underline{z}^{\prime},\underline{x}^{\prime})=\underline{z}^{\prime}

where the first identity type is inhabited by propositional
\Pi
-extensionality and the second by
\eta
-expansion.

Let us summarise this into the following:

Lemma 3.2.1
.

Let us assume that
\gamma:\Gamma
and
\delta:\Delta
are contexts and that
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
is a context homotopy equivalence between them. Moreover, let us assume that we are given judgements:

\succ

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}
and
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;B(\gamma,x):\textsc{Type}

\succ

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta:\Delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A^{\prime}(\delta):\textsc{Type}
and
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta:\Delta,\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;B^{\prime}(\delta,\underline{x}):\textsc{Type}

together with a homotopy equivalence
(f_{1};g_{1})
:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f_{1}(\gamma,x):A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}:A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g_{1}(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}):A(\gamma)

between
A(\gamma)
and
A^{\prime}(\delta)
relative to
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
, and a homotopy equivalence
(f_{2};g_{2})
:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma),y:B(\gamma,x)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f_{2}(\gamma,x,y):B^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma),f_{1}(\gamma,x))

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma),\underline{y}^{\prime}:B^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma),f_{1}(\gamma,x))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g_{2}(\gamma,x,\underline{y}^{\prime}):B(\gamma,x)

between
B(\gamma,x)
and
B^{\prime}(\delta,\underline{x})
relative to the extension
(\textnormal{{f}},\textnormal{{f}}_{m+1};\textnormal{{g}},\textnormal{{g}}_{n+1})
of
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
via
(f_{1};g_{1})
(see
Lemma

3.1.1
).

In context:

\gamma:\Gamma
and
z:\Pi_{x:A(\gamma)}B(\gamma,x)
and
\underline{z}^{\prime}:[\Pi_{\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta)}B^{\prime}(\delta,\underline{x})](\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))

if we name:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,z\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f^{\Pi}(\gamma,z)\equiv\lambda\underline{x}^{\prime}:A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))\;.\;q_{1}(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime})^{*}f_{2}(\gamma,g_{1}(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}),\textnormal{{ev}}(z,g_{1}(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}))):[\Pi_{\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta)}B^{\prime}(\delta,\underline{x})](\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,\underline{z}^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g^{\Pi}(\gamma,\underline{z}^{\prime})\equiv\lambda x:A(\gamma)\;.\;g_{2}(\gamma,x,\textnormal{{ev}}(\underline{z}^{\prime},f_{1}(\gamma,x))):\Pi_{x:A(\gamma)}B(\gamma,x)

then
(f^{\Pi};g^{\Pi})
is a homotopy equivalence between
\Pi_{x:A(\gamma)}B(\gamma,x)
and
\Pi_{\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta)}B^{\prime}(\delta,\underline{x})
relative to
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
.

3.3
Dependent sum of homotopy equivalences

As for
Section

3.2
, let us describe some:

Data.
Let us assume that we are given a context homotopy equivalence
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
between
\gamma:\Gamma
and
\delta:\Delta
and judgements:

\succ

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}
and
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;B(\gamma,x):\textsc{Type}

\succ

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta:\Delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A^{\prime}(\delta):\textsc{Type}
and
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta:\Delta,\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;B^{\prime}(\delta,\underline{x}):\textsc{Type}

together with a homotopy equivalence
(f_{1};g_{1})
:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f_{1}(\gamma,x):A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}:A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g_{1}(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}):A(\gamma)

between
A(\gamma)
and
A^{\prime}(\delta)
relative to
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
, and a homotopy equivalence
(f_{2};g_{2})
:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma),y:B(\gamma,x)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f_{2}(\gamma,x,y):B^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma),f_{1}(\gamma,x))

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma),\underline{y}^{\prime}:B^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma),f_{1}(\gamma,x))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g_{2}(\gamma,x,\underline{y}^{\prime}):B(\gamma,x)

between the types
B(\gamma,x)
and
B^{\prime}(\delta,\underline{x})
relative to the extension
(\textnormal{{f}},\textnormal{{f}}_{m+1};\textnormal{{g}},\textnormal{{g}}_{n+1})
of
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
via
(f_{1};g_{1})
(Lemma
3.1.1
).

Fact.
Again, we can use these data to construct a homotopy equivalence:

(f^{\Sigma};g^{\Sigma})

between
\Sigma_{x:A(\gamma)}B(\gamma,x)
and
\Sigma_{\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta)}B^{\prime}(\delta,\underline{x})
relative to
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
as follows, leading to Lemma
3.3.1
below.

As in
Section

3.2
, let us consider homotopies:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;p_{1}(\gamma,x):g_{1}(\gamma,f_{1}(\gamma,x))=x

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}:A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;q_{1}(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}):f_{1}(\gamma,g_{1}(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}))=\underline{x}^{\prime}

and homotopies:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma),y:B(\gamma,x)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;p_{2}(\gamma,x,y):g_{2}(\gamma,x,f_{2}(\gamma,x,y))=y

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma),\underline{y}^{\prime}:B^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma),f_{1}(\gamma,x))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;q_{2}(\gamma,x,\underline{y}^{\prime}):f_{2}(\gamma,x,g_{2}(\gamma,x,\underline{y}^{\prime}))=\underline{y}^{\prime}

as in our assumptions.

Let us fix the context
\gamma:\Gamma,u:\Sigma_{x:A(\gamma)}B(\gamma,x)
and let us observe that
\pi_{1}u:A(\gamma)
and that
\pi_{2}u:B(\gamma,\pi_{1}u)
. Therefore
f_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u):A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))
and
f_{2}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u,\pi_{2}u):B^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma),f_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u))
hence:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,u\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f^{\Sigma}(\gamma,u)\equiv\langle f_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u),f_{2}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u,\pi_{2}u)\rangle:[\Sigma_{\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta)}B^{\prime}(\delta,\underline{x})](\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma)).

Vice versa, if
\gamma:\Gamma,\underline{u}^{\prime}:\Sigma_{\underline{x}^{\prime}:A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))}B^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma),\underline{x}^{\prime})
then
\pi_{1}\underline{u}^{\prime}:A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))
hence
g_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}\underline{u}^{\prime}):A(\gamma)
. Moreover
\pi_{2}\underline{u}^{\prime}:B^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma),\pi_{1}\underline{u}^{\prime})
hence
q_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}\underline{u}^{\prime})^{*}\pi_{2}\underline{u}^{\prime}:B^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma),f_{1}(\gamma,g_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}\underline{u}^{\prime})))
. Therefore:

g_{2}(\gamma,g_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}\underline{u}^{\prime}),q_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}\underline{u}^{\prime})^{*}\pi_{2}\underline{u}^{\prime}):B(\gamma,g_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}\underline{u}^{\prime}))

hence:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,\underline{u}^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g^{\Sigma}(\gamma,\underline{u}^{\prime})\equiv\langle g_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}\underline{u}^{\prime}),g_{2}(\gamma,g_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}\underline{u}^{\prime}),q_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}\underline{u}^{\prime})^{*}\pi_{2}\underline{u}^{\prime})\rangle:\Sigma_{x:A(\gamma)}B(\gamma,x).

We claim that
(f^{\Sigma};g^{\Sigma})
is a homotopy equivalence between
\Sigma_{x:A(\gamma)}B(\gamma,x)
and
\Sigma_{\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta)}B^{\prime}(\delta,\underline{x})
relative to
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
. In order to verify that
g^{\Sigma}(\gamma,\underline{u}^{\prime})=u
, where
\underline{u}^{\prime}\equiv f^{\Sigma}(\gamma,u)
, it is enough to verify that:

p:g_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}\underline{u}^{\prime})=\pi_{1}u

g_{2}(\gamma,g_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}\underline{u}^{\prime}),q_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}\underline{u}^{\prime})^{*}\pi_{2}\underline{u}^{\prime})=p^{*}\pi_{2}u

as this implies that
g^{\Sigma}(\gamma,\underline{u}^{\prime})=\langle\pi_{1}u,\pi_{2}u\rangle=u
. Since:

g_{1}(\gamma,\beta_{1}(f_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u),f_{2}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u,\pi_{2}u)))\bullet p_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u):g_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}\underline{u}^{\prime})=\pi_{1}u

we are left to verify that:

g_{2}(\gamma,g_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}\underline{u}^{\prime}),q_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}\underline{u}^{\prime})^{*}\pi_{2}\underline{u}^{\prime})=p^{*}\pi_{2}u

for
p\equiv g_{1}(\gamma,\beta_{1}(f_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u),f_{2}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u,\pi_{2}u)))\bullet p_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u)
. Let us observe that the following square:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{1}(\gamma,g_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}\underline{u}^{\prime}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{1}(\gamma,g_{1}(\gamma,f_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u)))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}\underline{u}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{1}(\gamma\text{,}\pi_{1}\underline{u}^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{1}(\gamma\text{,}g_{1}(\gamma\text{,}\beta_{1}(f_{1}(\gamma\text{,}\pi_{1}u)\text{,}f_{2}(\gamma\text{,}\pi_{1}u\text{,}\pi_{2}u))))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{1}(\gamma\text{,}f_{1}(\gamma\text{,}\pi_{1}u))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta_{1}(f_{1}(\gamma\text{,}\pi_{1}u)\text{,}f_{2}(\gamma\text{,}\pi_{1}u\text{,}\pi_{2}u))}

commutes propositionally, because
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;q_{1}(\gamma,-):f_{1}(\gamma,g_{1}(\gamma,-))=-
is a homotopy. Therefore
q_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}\underline{u}^{\prime})=

\displaystyle=f_{1}(\gamma,g_{1}(\gamma,\beta_{1}(f_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u),f_{2}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u,\pi_{2}u))))\bullet q_{1}(\gamma,f_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u))\bullet\beta_{1}(f_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u),f_{2}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u,\pi_{2}u))^{-1}

\displaystyle=f_{1}(\gamma,g_{1}(\gamma,\beta_{1}(f_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u),f_{2}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u,\pi_{2}u))))\bullet f_{1}(\gamma,p_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u))\bullet\beta_{1}(f_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u),f_{2}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u,\pi_{2}u))^{-1}

\displaystyle=f_{1}(\gamma,g_{1}(\gamma,\beta_{1}(f_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u),f_{2}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u,\pi_{2}u)))\bullet p_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u))\bullet\beta_{1}(f_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u),f_{2}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u,\pi_{2}u))^{-1}

\displaystyle\equiv f_{1}(\gamma,p)\bullet\beta_{1}(f_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u),f_{2}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u,\pi_{2}u))^{-1}

where the second equality follows because
q_{1}(\gamma,f_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u))=f_{1}(\gamma,p_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u))
without loss of generality and the third by propositional functoriality. By propositional functoriality and groupoidality, we deduce that:

\displaystyle q_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}\underline{u}^{\prime})^{*}\pi_{2}\underline{u}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=f_{1}(\gamma,p)^{*}(\beta_{1}(f_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u),f_{2}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u,\pi_{2}u))^{-1})^{*}\pi_{2}\underline{u}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=f_{1}(\gamma,p)^{*}f_{2}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u,\pi_{2}u)

hence:

g_{2}(\gamma,g_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}\underline{u}^{\prime}),q_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}\underline{u}^{\prime})^{*}\pi_{2}\underline{u}^{\prime})=g_{2}(\gamma,g_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}\underline{u}^{\prime}),f_{1}(\gamma,p)^{*}f_{2}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u,\pi_{2}u)).

Therefore we are left to observe that:

g_{2}(\gamma,g_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}\underline{u}^{\prime}),f_{1}(\gamma,p)^{*}f_{2}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u,\pi_{2}u))=p^{*}\pi_{2}u

which follows by the judgement:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ p:x_{1}=x_{2},y:B(\gamma,x_{2})\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g_{2}(\gamma,x_{1},f_{1}(\gamma,p)^{*}f_{2}(\gamma,x_{2},y))=p^{*}y

with
x_{1}\equiv g_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}\underline{u}^{\prime})
,
x_{2}\equiv\pi_{1}u
and
y\equiv\pi_{2}u
. This last judgement is true by path elimination on
p
and since
p_{2}(\gamma,x_{1},y):g_{2}(\gamma,x_{1},f_{2}(\gamma,x_{1},y))=y
.

Vice versa, in order to verify that
f^{\Sigma}(\gamma,u)=\underline{u}^{\prime}
, where
u\equiv g^{\Sigma}(\gamma,\underline{u}^{\prime})
, let us observe that:

q\equiv f_{1}(\gamma,\beta_{1}(\overline{g}_{1}(\gamma,\underline{u}^{\prime}),\overline{g}_{2}(\gamma,\underline{u}^{\prime})))\bullet q_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}\underline{u}^{\prime}):f_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u)=\pi_{1}\underline{u}^{\prime}

where:

\displaystyle\overline{g}_{1}(\gamma,\underline{u}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\equiv g_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}\underline{u}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\overline{g}_{2}(\gamma,\underline{u}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\equiv g_{2}(\gamma,g_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}\underline{u}^{\prime}),q_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}\underline{u}^{\prime})^{*}\pi_{2}\underline{u}^{\prime}).

Hence we are left to verify that:

f_{2}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u,\pi_{2}u)=q^{*}\pi_{2}\underline{u}^{\prime}

as this implies that
f^{\Sigma}(\gamma,u)=\langle\pi_{1}\underline{u}^{\prime},\pi_{2}\underline{u}^{\prime}\rangle=\underline{u}^{\prime}
. This is the case, as:

\displaystyle f_{2}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u,\pi_{2}u)

\displaystyle=f_{1}(\gamma,\beta_{1}(\overline{g}_{1}(\gamma,\underline{u}^{\prime}),\overline{g}_{2}(\gamma,\underline{u}^{\prime})))^{*}f_{2}(\gamma,\overline{g}_{1}(\gamma,\underline{u}^{\prime}),\overline{g}_{2}(\gamma,\underline{u}^{\prime}))

\displaystyle=f_{1}(\gamma,\beta_{1}(\overline{g}_{1}(\gamma,\underline{u}^{\prime}),\overline{g}_{2}(\gamma,\underline{u}^{\prime})))^{*}q_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}\underline{u}^{\prime})^{*}\pi_{2}\underline{u}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=(\;f_{1}(\gamma,\beta_{1}(\overline{g}_{1}(\gamma,\underline{u}^{\prime}),\overline{g}_{2}(\gamma,\underline{u}^{\prime})))\bullet q_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}\underline{u}^{\prime})\;)^{*}\pi_{2}\underline{u}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\equiv q^{*}\pi_{2}\underline{u}^{\prime}

where the second equality holds by propositional functoriality and since:

q_{2}(\gamma,g_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}\underline{u}^{\prime}),q_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}\underline{u}^{\prime})^{*}\pi_{2}\underline{u}^{\prime}):f_{2}(\gamma,\overline{g}_{1}(\gamma,\underline{u}^{\prime}),\overline{g}_{2}(\gamma,\underline{u}^{\prime}))=q_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}\underline{u}^{\prime})^{*}\pi_{2}\underline{u}^{\prime}

and the third by propositional functoriality: we are left to verify the first. Since:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ x_{1},x_{2}:A(\gamma),y_{1}:B(\gamma,x_{1}),y_{2}:B(\gamma,x_{2}),p:x_{1}=x_{2},q:y_{1}=p^{*}y_{2}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;

f_{2}(\gamma,x_{1},y_{1})=f_{1}(\gamma,p)^{*}f_{2}(\gamma,x_{2},y_{2})

by path elimination on
p
and
q
, we are done if:

p\equiv\beta_{1}(\overline{g}_{1}(\gamma,\underline{u}^{\prime}),\overline{g}_{2}(\gamma,\underline{u}^{\prime}))\text{ and }q\equiv\beta_{2}(\overline{g}_{1}(\gamma,\underline{u}^{\prime}),\overline{g}_{2}(\gamma,\underline{u}^{\prime})).

Let us summarise the present subsection into the following:

Lemma 3.3.1
.

Let us assume that
\gamma:\Gamma
and
\delta:\Delta
are contexts and that
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
is a context homotopy equivalence between them. Moreover, let us assume that we are given judgements:

\succ

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}
and
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;B(\gamma,x):\textsc{Type}

\succ

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta:\Delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A^{\prime}(\delta):\textsc{Type}
and
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta:\Delta,\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;B^{\prime}(\delta,\underline{x}):\textsc{Type}

together with a homotopy equivalence
(f_{1};g_{1})
:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f_{1}(\gamma,x):A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}:A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g_{1}(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}):A(\gamma)

between
A(\gamma)
and
A^{\prime}(\delta)
relative to
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
, and a homotopy equivalence
(f_{2};g_{2})
:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma),y:B(\gamma,x)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f_{2}(\gamma,x,y):B^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma),f_{1}(\gamma,x))

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma),\underline{y}^{\prime}:B^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma),f_{1}(\gamma,x))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g_{2}(\gamma,x,\underline{y}^{\prime}):B(\gamma,x)

between
B(\gamma,x)
and
B^{\prime}(\delta,\underline{x})
relative to the extension
(\textnormal{{f}},\textnormal{{f}}_{m+1};\textnormal{{g}},\textnormal{{g}}_{n+1})
of
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
via
(f_{1};g_{1})
(Lemma
3.1.1
).

In context:

\gamma:\Gamma
and
u:\Sigma_{x:A(\gamma)}B(\gamma,x)
and
\underline{u}^{\prime}:[\Sigma_{\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta)}B^{\prime}(\delta,\underline{x})](\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))

if we name:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,u\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f^{\Sigma}(\gamma,u)\equiv\langle f_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u),f_{2}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u,\pi_{2}u)\rangle:[\Sigma_{\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta)}B^{\prime}(\delta,\underline{x})](\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,\underline{u}^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g^{\Sigma}(\gamma,\underline{u}^{\prime})\equiv\langle g_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}\underline{u}^{\prime}),g_{2}(\gamma,g_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}\underline{u}^{\prime}),q_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}\underline{u}^{\prime})^{*}\pi_{2}\underline{u}^{\prime})\rangle:\Sigma_{x:A(\gamma)}B(\gamma,x)

then
(f^{\Sigma};g^{\Sigma})
is a homotopy equivalence between
\Sigma_{x:A(\gamma)}B(\gamma,x)
and
\Sigma_{\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta)}B^{\prime}(\delta,\underline{x})
relative to
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
.

3.4
Identity types over homotopy equivalent types

Again, let us describe some:

Data.
Let us assume that we are given a context homotopy equivalence
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
between
\gamma:\Gamma
and
\delta:\Delta
and judgements:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}\textnormal{ and }{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta:\Delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A^{\prime}(\delta):\textsc{Type}

together with a homotopy equivalence
(f;g)
:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\gamma,x):A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}:A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}):A(\gamma)

between
A(\gamma)
and
A^{\prime}(\delta)
relative to
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
. Moreover let us assume that we are given judgements:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;s_{1}(\gamma),s_{2}(\gamma):A(\gamma)

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta:\Delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;t_{1}(\delta),t_{2}(\delta):A^{\prime}(\delta)

together with:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{r}}_{1}(\gamma):f(\gamma,s_{1}(\gamma))=t_{1}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{r}}_{2}(\gamma):f(\gamma,s_{2}(\gamma))=t_{2}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma)).

Fact.
We can define a homotopy equivalence
(f^{=};g^{=})
between
s_{1}(\gamma)=s_{2}(\gamma)
and
t_{1}(\delta)=t_{2}(\delta)
relative to
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
as follows, leading to Lemma
3.4.1
below.

Let us consider homotopies:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;p(\gamma,x):g(\gamma,f(\gamma,x))=x

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}:A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;q(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}):f(\gamma,g(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}))=\underline{x}^{\prime}

We define:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,p:s_{1}(\gamma)=s_{2}(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f^{=}(\gamma,p)\equiv\textnormal{{r}}_{1}(\gamma)^{-1}\bullet f(\gamma,p)\bullet\textnormal{{r}}_{2}(\gamma):t_{1}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))=t_{2}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))

and:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,\underline{p}^{\prime}:t_{1}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))=t_{2}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;

{g^{=}(\gamma,\overline{p}^{\prime})\equiv p(\gamma,s_{1}(\gamma))^{-1}\bullet g(\gamma,\textnormal{{r}}_{1}(\gamma)\bullet\underline{p}^{\prime}\bullet\textnormal{{r}}_{2}(\gamma)^{-1})\bullet p(\gamma,s_{2}(\gamma)):s_{1}(\gamma)=s_{2}(\gamma)}.

Let us observe that:

g^{=}(\gamma,f^{=}(\gamma,p))=p(\gamma,s_{1}(\gamma))^{-1}\bullet g(\gamma,f(\gamma,p))\bullet p(\gamma,s_{2}(\gamma))=p

where the first identity type is inhabited by groupoidality and the second since:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;p(\gamma,-):g(\gamma,f(\gamma,-))=-.

Moreover, by propositional functoriality:

f^{=}(\gamma,g^{=}(\gamma,\underline{p}^{\prime}))=a(\gamma)\bullet f(\gamma,g(\gamma,\underline{p}^{\prime}))\bullet b(\gamma)

where:

\displaystyle a(\gamma)

\displaystyle\equiv\textnormal{{r}}_{1}(\gamma)^{-1}\bullet f(\gamma,p(\gamma,s_{1}(\gamma)))^{-1}\bullet f(\gamma,g(\gamma,\textnormal{{r}}_{1}(\gamma)))

\displaystyle=\textnormal{{r}}_{1}(\gamma)^{-1}\bullet q(\gamma,f(\gamma,s_{1}(\gamma)))^{-1}\bullet f(\gamma,g(\gamma,\textnormal{{r}}_{1}(\gamma)))

\displaystyle b(\gamma)

\displaystyle\equiv f(\gamma,g(\gamma,\textnormal{{r}}_{2}(\gamma)))^{-1}\bullet f(\gamma,p(\gamma,s_{2}(\gamma)))\bullet\textnormal{{r}}_{2}(\gamma)

\displaystyle=f(\gamma,g(\gamma,\textnormal{{r}}_{2}(\gamma)))^{-1}\bullet q(\gamma,f(\gamma,s_{2}(\gamma)))\bullet\textnormal{{r}}_{2}(\gamma)

where the identity types are inhabited because
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\gamma,p(\gamma,x))=q(\gamma,f(\gamma,x))
without loss of generality. Observe that:

\displaystyle a(\gamma)

\displaystyle=q(\gamma,t_{1}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma)))^{-1}

\displaystyle b(\gamma)

\displaystyle=q(\gamma,t_{2}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma)))

as the diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f(\gamma,s_{i}(\gamma))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t_{i}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f(\gamma,g(\gamma,f(\gamma,s_{i}(\gamma))))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f(\gamma,g(\gamma,t_{i}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{{r}}_{i}(\gamma)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q(\gamma\text{,}f(\gamma\text{,}s_{i}(\gamma)))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f(\gamma\text{,}g(\gamma\text{,}\textnormal{{r}}_{i}(\gamma)))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q(\gamma\text{,}t_{i}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma)))}

commutes propositionally for
i=1,2
, since
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;q(\gamma,-):f(\gamma,g(\gamma,-))=-
. We conclude that:

f^{=}(\gamma,g^{=}(\gamma,\underline{p}^{\prime}))=q(\gamma,t_{1}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma)))^{-1}\bullet f(\gamma,g(\gamma,\underline{p}^{\prime}))\bullet q(\gamma,t_{2}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma)))=\underline{p}^{\prime}

by groupoidality and since
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;q(\gamma,-):f(\gamma,g(\gamma,-))=-
. We summarise this fact into the following:

Lemma 3.4.1
.

Let us assume that we are given a context homotopy equivalence
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
between
\gamma:\Gamma
and
\delta:\Delta
and judgements:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}\textnormal{ and }{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta:\Delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A^{\prime}(\delta):\textsc{Type}

together with a homotopy equivalence
(f;g)
:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\gamma,x):A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}:A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}):A(\gamma)

between
A(\gamma)
and
A^{\prime}(\delta)
relative to
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
. Moreover let us assume that we are given judgements:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;s_{1}(\gamma),s_{2}(\gamma):A(\gamma)

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta:\Delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;t_{1}(\delta),t_{2}(\delta):A^{\prime}(\delta)

together with:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{r}}_{1}(\gamma):f(\gamma,s_{1}(\gamma))=t_{1}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{r}}_{2}(\gamma):f(\gamma,s_{2}(\gamma))=t_{2}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma)).

If we name:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,p:s_{1}(\gamma)=s_{2}(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f^{=}(\gamma,p)\equiv\textnormal{{r}}_{1}(\gamma)^{-1}\bullet f(\gamma,p)\bullet\textnormal{{r}}_{2}(\gamma):t_{1}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))=t_{2}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,\underline{p}^{\prime}:t_{1}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))=t_{2}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g^{=}(\gamma,\underline{p}^{\prime})\equiv p(\gamma,s_{1}(\gamma))^{-1}\bullet g(\gamma,\textnormal{{r}}_{1}(\gamma)\bullet\underline{p}^{\prime}\bullet\textnormal{{r}}_{2}(\gamma)^{-1})\bullet p(\gamma,s_{2}(\gamma)):s_{1}(\gamma)=s_{2}(\gamma)

then
(f^{=};g^{=})
is a homotopy equivalence between
s_{1}(\gamma)=s_{2}(\gamma)
and
t_{1}(\delta)=t_{2}(\delta)
relative to
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
.

3.5
Canonical homotopy equivalences between types

In
Section

3.6
we are going to use
Lemma

3.1.1
to define a specific class of context homotopy equivalences. However, in order to do so, we first need to identify a particular notion of homotopy equivalence between types relative to a given context homotopy equivalence: we are going to apply
Lemma

3.1.1
to these ones only. In this subsection we inductively present this notion.

Let
\gamma:\Gamma
and
\delta:\Delta
be contexts together with a context homotopy equivalence
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
and let
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;S(\gamma):\textsc{Type}
and
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta:\Delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;T(\delta):\textsc{Type}
have h-propositional identities. We provide a list of inductive clauses determining the family of the
canonical homotopy equivalences

\langle\;\varphi\;|\;\psi\;\rangle
between
S(\gamma)
and
T(\delta)
relative to
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
:

(a)

If

S(\gamma)\equiv S
and
T(\delta)\equiv S(\gamma)\equiv S
, then the identity of
S
(as judgement of the form
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,s:S\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;s:S
) and itself
constitute a canonical homotopy equivalence
\langle\;\varphi\;|\;\psi\;\rangle
between
S(\gamma)
and
T(\delta)
relative to
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
. Observe that this homotopy equivalence between
S
and itself is in fact
relative to

(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
, as:

–

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,s:S\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\varphi(\gamma,s)\equiv s:S\equiv S(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))

–

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,s:S\equiv S(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\psi(\gamma,s)\equiv s:S
.

(b)

If
S(\gamma)\equiv\Pi_{x:A(\gamma)}B(\gamma,x)
and
T(\delta)\equiv\Pi_{\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta)}B^{\prime}(\delta,\underline{x})
for some judgements:

–

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}
and
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;B(\gamma,x):\textsc{Type}

–

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta:\Delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A^{\prime}(\delta):\textsc{Type}
and
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta:\Delta,\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;B^{\prime}(\delta,\underline{x}):\textsc{Type}

and if there are a canonical homotopy equivalence
\langle\;f_{1}\;|\;g_{1}\;\rangle
:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f_{1}(\gamma,x):A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}:A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g_{1}(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}):A(\gamma)

between
A(\gamma)
and
A^{\prime}(\delta)
relative to
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
and a canonical homotopy equivalence
\langle\;f_{2}\;|\;g_{2}\;\rangle
:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma),y:B(\gamma,x)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f_{2}(\gamma,x,y):B^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma),f_{1}(\gamma,x))

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma),\underline{y}^{\prime}:B^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma),f_{1}(\gamma,x))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g_{2}(\gamma,x,\underline{y}^{\prime}):B(\gamma,x)

between
B(\gamma,x)
and
B^{\prime}(\delta,\underline{x})
relative to the extension
(\textnormal{{f}},\textnormal{{f}}_{m+1};\textnormal{{g}},\textnormal{{g}}_{n+1})
of
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
via
(f_{1};g_{1})
(Lemma
3.1.1
), then the homotopy equivalence
(f^{\Pi};g^{\Pi})
of Lemma
3.2.1
is a canonical homotopy equivalence
\langle\;\varphi\;|\;\psi\;\rangle
between
S(\gamma)
and
T(\delta)
relative to
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
.

(c)

If
S(\gamma)\equiv\Sigma_{x:A(\gamma)}B(\gamma,x)
and
T(\delta)\equiv\Sigma_{\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta)}B^{\prime}(\delta,\underline{x})
for some judgements:

–

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}
and
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;B(\gamma,x):\textsc{Type}

–

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta:\Delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A^{\prime}(\delta):\textsc{Type}
and
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta:\Delta,\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;B^{\prime}(\delta,\underline{x}):\textsc{Type}

and if there are a canonical homotopy equivalence
\langle\;f_{1}\;|\;g_{1}\;\rangle
:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f_{1}(\gamma,x):A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}:A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g_{1}(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}):A(\gamma)

between
A(\gamma)
and
A^{\prime}(\delta)
relative to
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
and a canonical homotopy equivalence
\langle\;f_{2}\;|\;g_{2}\;\rangle
:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma),y:B(\gamma,x)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f_{2}(\gamma,x,y):B^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma),f_{1}(\gamma,x))

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma),\underline{y}^{\prime}:B^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma),f_{1}(\gamma,x))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g_{2}(\gamma,x,\underline{y}^{\prime}):B(\gamma,x)

between
B(\gamma,x)
and
B^{\prime}(\delta,\underline{x})
relative to the extension
(\textnormal{{f}},\textnormal{{f}}_{m+1};\textnormal{{g}},\textnormal{{g}}_{n+1})
of
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
via
(f_{1};g_{1})
(Lemma
3.1.1
), then the homotopy equivalence
(f^{\Sigma};g^{\Sigma})
of Lemma
3.3.1
is a canonical homotopy equivalence
\langle\;\varphi\;|\;\psi\;\rangle
between
S(\gamma)
and
T(\delta)
relative to
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
.

(d)

If
S(\gamma)\equiv s_{1}(\gamma)=s_{2}(\gamma)
and
T(\delta)\equiv t_{1}(\delta)=t_{2}(\delta)
for some judgements:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}
and
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta:\Delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A^{\prime}(\delta):\textsc{Type}

and some judgements:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;s_{1}(\gamma),s_{2}(\gamma):A(\gamma)
and
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta:\Delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;t_{1}(\delta),t_{2}(\delta):A^{\prime}(\delta)

and if there are a canonical homotopy equivalence
\langle\;f\;|\;g\;\rangle
:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\gamma,x):A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}:A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}):A(\gamma)

between
A(\gamma)
and
A^{\prime}(\delta)
relative to
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
and judgements:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{r}}_{1}(\gamma):f(\gamma,s_{1}(\gamma))=t_{1}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{r}}_{2}(\gamma):f(\gamma,s_{2}(\gamma))=t_{2}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma)).

then the homotopy equivalence
(f^{=},g^{=})
of Lemma
3.4.1
is a canonical homotopy equivalence
\langle\;\varphi\;|\;\psi\;\rangle
between
S(\gamma)
and
T(\delta)
relative to
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
.

3.6
Canonical homotopy equivalences between contexts

In this subsection we recursively define a family of context homotopy equivalences that we refer to as the
canonical
ones. In fact, when we make the syntax of
PTT
into a model of
ETT
, we need to define equivalence classes of contexts modulo the context homotopy equivalences of this particular shape that we are going to identify.

As mentioned before, this proof strategy, based on defining a family of canonical equivalences between contexts - up to which contexts are identified - appears in a work by Hofmann
[
Hof95a
,
Hof96
]
in order to obtain an analogous conservativity result. Moreover, such a notion of canonical equivalence appears - in a similar formulation - in a work by Maietti
[
Mai09
]
in order to define an interpretation of the extensional level of Minimalist Foundation within the intensional one, and in one by Contente and Maietti
[
CM22
]
in order to interpret the former within Homotopy Type Theory.

Let
\gamma:\Gamma
and
\delta:\Delta
be two contexts. We give a list of inductive clauses determining the family of the
canonical context homotopy equivalences

\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
between them:

(1)

If
\gamma:\Gamma
and
\delta:\Delta
are the empty context then the empty list (as context morphism
\Gamma\to\Delta
) and itself (as context morphism
\Delta\to\Gamma
) constitute a canonical context homotopy equivalence
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
.

(2)

If
\gamma:\Gamma
and
\delta:\Delta
are of the form
\gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime},x:A(\gamma^{\prime})
and
\delta^{\prime}:\Delta^{\prime},\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime})
respectively and:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma^{\prime}):\Delta^{\prime}

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime}:\Delta^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{g}}(\delta^{\prime}):\Gamma^{\prime}

is a canonical context homotopy equivalence
\langle\;\textnormal{{f}}\;|\;\textnormal{{g}}\;\rangle
and:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime},x:A(\gamma^{\prime})\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\gamma^{\prime},x):A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma^{\prime}))

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime},\underline{x}^{\prime}:A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma^{\prime}))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g(\gamma^{\prime},\underline{x}^{\prime}):A(\gamma^{\prime})

is a canonical homotopy equivalence
\langle\;f\;|\;g\;\rangle
between
A(\gamma^{\prime})
and
A^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime})
relative to
\langle\;\textnormal{{f}}\;|\;\textnormal{{g}}\;\rangle
(in the sense of
Section

3.5
) then the extension:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime},x:A(\gamma^{\prime})\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;(\textnormal{{f}},\textnormal{{f}}_{m+1})(\gamma^{\prime},x):\Delta^{\prime},A^{\prime}

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime}:\Delta^{\prime},\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime})\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;(\textnormal{{g}},\textnormal{{g}}_{n+1})(\delta^{\prime},\underline{x}):\Gamma^{\prime},A

of
\langle\;\textnormal{{f}}\;|\;\textnormal{{g}}\;\rangle
via
\langle\;f\;|\;g\;\rangle
(
Lemma

3.1.1
) is a canonical context homotopy equivalence
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
.

We end the current section with the following:

Remark 3.6.1
.

Let
A:\textsc{Type}
,
a,b:A
, and
q:a=b
be atomic judgements of PTT. By
(a)
of
Section

3.5
, the pair:

\langle\;\;\;{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ x:A\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;x:A\;\;\;|\;\;\;{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ x:A\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;x:A\;\;\;\rangle

constitutes a canonical homotopy equivalence between
A
and itself relative to the canonical context homotopy equivalence of
(1)
. Therefore, by
(b)
of
Section

3.5
, the pair:

\langle\;\;\;{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ p:a=a\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{r}}(a)^{-1}\bullet p\bullet q:a=b\;\;\;|\;\;\;{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ p^{\prime}:a=b\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{r}}(a)\bullet p^{\prime}\bullet q^{-1}:a=a\;\;\;\rangle

constitutes a canonical homotopy equivalence between the types
a=a
and
a=b
relative to the canonical context homotopy equivalence of
(1)
. By
(2)
we obtain a canonical context homotopy equivalence:

\langle\;\;\;{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ p:a=a\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{r}}(a)^{-1}\bullet p\bullet q:a=b\;\;\;|\;\;\;{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ p^{\prime}:a=b\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{r}}(a)\bullet p^{\prime}\bullet q^{-1}:a=a\;\;\;\rangle

between the context
p:a=a
and the context
p^{\prime}:a=b
.

However, assuming that
a\equiv b
(and w.l.o.g. that
p^{\prime}\equiv p
) this homotopy equivalence is homotopic - i.e. propositionally equal - to the identity over the context
p:a=a
if and only if the type
q=\textnormal{{r}}(a)
is inhabited - cf.
Proposition

4.2.5
on contexts with h-propositional identities.

We will use this fundamental canonical context homotopy equivalence, which we have presented here in a particular case, in
Section

6.2
to show that the syntax of
PTT
with contexts identified modulo these canonical equivalences constitutes a model of extensional identity types.

4
Properties of the canonical homotopy equivalences

In this section we prove those properties of the family of the canonical context homotopy equivalences (defined in
Section

3.6
) that are needed in order to define a model of
ETT
starting from the syntax of
PTT
. Our approach is the following: we start by proving properties (e.g. the fact that an equivalence relation is induced between the contexts of
PTT
) for the general family of canonical equivalences; secondly, we restrict to a smaller family of contexts, called
contexts with h-propositional identities
, and we only consider canonical equivalences between them, so that additional properties (e.g. the uniqueness - up to homotopy - of a canonical equivalence between two given contexts) are satisfied.

4.1
Properties of the family of canonical equivalences

We remind that we use the notation
(\;\cdot\;;\;\cdot\;)
to indicate any homotopy equivalence between contexts or types in context, reserving the symbol
\langle\;\cdot\;|\;\cdot\;\rangle
for the canonical ones. We start our list of results from the following observation:

Lemma 4.1.1
.

Let
\gamma:\Gamma
be a context
\gamma_{1}:\Gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}:\Gamma_{2}(\gamma_{1}),...,\gamma_{n}:\Gamma_{n}(\gamma_{1},...,\gamma_{n-1})
and let
\delta:\Delta
be a context
\delta_{1}:\Delta_{1},\delta_{2}:\Delta_{2}(\delta_{1}),...,\delta_{m}:\Delta_{m}(\delta_{1},...,\delta_{m-1})
. If there is a canonical context homotopy equivalence
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
between
\gamma:\Gamma
and
\delta:\Delta
then
n=m
.

Proof.

By induction on the complexity of
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
. If
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
is of the form
(1)
then
n=0=m
and we are done. If
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
is of the form
(2)
for some canonical context homotopy equivalence
\langle\;\textnormal{{f}}\;|\;\textnormal{{g}}\;\rangle
and some canonical homotopy equivalence
\langle\;f\;|\;g\;\rangle
relative to
\langle\;\textnormal{{f}}\;|\;\textnormal{{g}}\;\rangle
, then by inductive hypothesis
n-1=m-1
hence we are done.
∎

Secondly, we prove the properties of reflexivity, symmetry and transitivity of the relation between contexts induced by the family of the canonical equivalences between them.

Proposition 4.1.2

(Reflexivity)
.

Let
\gamma:\Gamma
be a context
\gamma_{1}:\Gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}:\Gamma_{2}(\gamma_{1}),...,\gamma_{n}:\Gamma_{n}(\gamma_{1},...,\gamma_{n-1})
. Then there is a canonical context homotopy equivalence
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
from
\gamma
to
\gamma
such that
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{c}}(\gamma)=\gamma
( i.e.
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{d}}(\gamma)=\gamma
).

Proof.

By induction on the length
n
of
\gamma:\Gamma
. If
n=0
then we are done by
(1)
. Otherwise, let
\gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime}
be the context
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},...,\gamma_{n-1}
. By inductive hypothesis, there is a canonical context homotopy equivalence
\langle\;\textnormal{{f}}\;|\;\textnormal{{g}}\;\rangle
between
\gamma^{\prime}
and itself such that
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\alpha(\gamma^{\prime}):\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma^{\prime})=\gamma^{\prime}
( i.e.
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{g}}(\gamma^{\prime})=\gamma^{\prime}
).

Let us pretend that we know that there is a canonical homotopy equivalence
\langle\;f\;|\;g\;\rangle
between
\Gamma_{n}(\gamma^{\prime})
and itself relative to
\langle\;\textnormal{{f}}\;|\;\textnormal{{g}}\;\rangle
and such that
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime},\gamma_{n}:\Gamma_{n}(\gamma^{\prime})\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\gamma^{\prime},\gamma_{n})=\alpha(\gamma^{\prime})^{*}\gamma_{n}
( i.e.
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime},\gamma_{n}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g(\gamma^{\prime},\alpha(\gamma^{\prime})^{*}\gamma_{n})=\gamma_{n}
). Then the extension:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime},\gamma_{n}:\Gamma_{n}(\gamma^{\prime})\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;(\textnormal{{f}},\textnormal{{f}}_{m+1})(\gamma^{\prime},\gamma_{n}):\Gamma^{\prime},\Gamma_{n}

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime},\gamma_{n}:\Gamma_{n}(\gamma^{\prime})\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;(\textnormal{{g}},\textnormal{{g}}_{n+1})(\gamma^{\prime},\gamma_{n}):\Gamma^{\prime},\Gamma_{n}

of
\langle\;\textnormal{{f}}\;|\;\textnormal{{g}}\;\rangle
via
\langle\;f\;|\;g\;\rangle
(Lemma
3.1.1
) satisfies
(\textnormal{{f}},\textnormal{{f}}_{m+1})(\gamma)=\gamma
( hence
(\textnormal{{g}},\textnormal{{g}}_{n+1})(\gamma)=\gamma
). Moreover, it is a canonical homotopy equivalence
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
by
(2)
.

Hence we are done if there is a canonical homotopy equivalence
\langle\;f\;|\;g\;\rangle
between
\Gamma_{n}(\gamma^{\prime})
and itself relative to
\langle\;\textnormal{{f}}\;|\;\textnormal{{g}}\;\rangle
and such that
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime},\gamma_{n}:\Gamma_{n}(\gamma^{\prime})\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\gamma^{\prime},\gamma_{n})=\alpha(\gamma^{\prime})^{*}\gamma_{n}
. But this is true because of the following Lemma
4.1.3
.
∎

Lemma 4.1.3
.

Let
\gamma:\Gamma
be a context and let
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
be a context homotopy equivalence between
\gamma
and itself such that
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\alpha(\gamma):\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma)=\gamma
( and equivalently
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{g}}(\gamma)=\gamma
). Moreover, let us assume that
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;S(\gamma):\textsc{Type}
. Then there exists a canonical homotopy equivalence
\langle\;\varphi\;|\;\psi\;\rangle
between
S(\gamma)
and itself relative to
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
and such that:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,s:S(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\varphi(\gamma,s)=\alpha(\gamma)^{*}s
( i.e.
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,s\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\psi(\gamma,\alpha(\gamma)^{*}s)=s
).

Sketch of proof. See
Appendix

A
for more details.

By induction on the complexity of the type
S(\gamma)
.

If
S(\gamma)\equiv S
then by
(a)
the pair:

({\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,s:S\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\varphi(\gamma,s)\equiv s:S;\;{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,s:S\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\psi(\gamma,s)\equiv s:S)

is a canonical homotopy equivalence between
S
and itself relative to
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
. Moreover, let us observe that:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,s\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\varphi(\gamma,s)\equiv s=\alpha(\gamma)^{*}s

-by (generalised) path induction on
\alpha(\gamma)
- hence we are done.

If
S(\gamma)
is either of the form
\Pi_{x:A(\gamma)}B(\gamma,x)
or of the form
\Sigma_{x:A(\gamma)}B(\gamma,x)
for some judgements
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}
and
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;B(\gamma,x):\textsc{Type}
, then by inductive hypothesis there are a canonical homotopy equivalence
\langle\;f_{1}\;|\;g_{1}\;\rangle
between
A(\gamma)
and itself relative to
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
such that
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\alpha_{1}(\gamma,x):f_{1}(\gamma,x)=\alpha(\gamma)^{*}x
(and equivalently
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g_{1}(\gamma,\alpha(\gamma)^{*}x)=x
) and a canonical homotopy equivalence
\langle\;f_{2}\;|\;g_{2}\;\rangle
between
B(\gamma,x)
and itself relative to the extension
(\textnormal{{f}},\textnormal{{f}}_{m+1};\textnormal{{g}},\textnormal{{g}}_{n+1})
of
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
via
(f_{1};g_{1})
(Lemma
3.1.1
) such that
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x,y\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f_{2}(\gamma,x,y)=(\alpha(\gamma),\alpha_{1}(\gamma,x))^{*}y
(and equivalently
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x,y\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g_{2}(\gamma,x,(\alpha(\gamma),\alpha_{1}(\gamma,x))^{*}y)=y
). The homotopy equivalence
(f^{\Pi};g^{\Pi})
of Lemma
3.2.1
is a canonical homotopy equivalence relative to
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
. One is therefore left to verify that:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,z:\Pi_{x:A(\gamma)}B(\gamma,x)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g^{\Pi}(\gamma,\alpha(\gamma)^{*}z)=z

and that:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,u:\Sigma_{x:A(\gamma)}B(\gamma,x)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f^{\Sigma}(\gamma,u)\equiv\langle f_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u),f_{2}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u,\pi_{2}u)\rangle=\alpha(\gamma)^{*}u

where
(f^{\Pi};g^{\Pi})
is the canonical homotopy equivalence relative to
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
of Lemma
3.2.1
and
(f^{\Sigma};g^{\Sigma})
is the canonical homotopy equivalence relative to
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
of Lemma
3.3.1
.

One reasons similarly if
S(\gamma)
is of the form
s_{1}(\gamma)=s_{2}(\gamma)
for some judgement
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}
.
∎

Proposition 4.1.4

(Symmetry)
.

If
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
is a canonical context homotopy equivalence between
\gamma:\Gamma
and
\delta:\Delta
, then there is a canonical context homotopy equivalence
\langle\;\textnormal{{d}}^{\prime}\;|\;\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}\;\rangle
between
\delta
and
\gamma
such that
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{c}}(\gamma)=\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}(\gamma)
and equivalently
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta:\Delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{d}}(\delta)=\textnormal{{d}}^{\prime}(\delta)
.

Proof.

By induction on the complexity of
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
. If
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
is of the form
(1)
then we are done. If
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
is of the form
(2)
for some canonical context homotopy equivalence
\langle\;\textnormal{{f}}\;|\;\textnormal{{g}}\;\rangle
between
\gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime}
and
\delta^{\prime}:\Delta^{\prime}
and some canonical homotopy equivalence
\langle\;f\;|\;g\;\rangle
between
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma^{\prime})
and
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime})
relative to
\langle\;\textnormal{{f}}\;|\;\textnormal{{g}}\;\rangle

- hence
\gamma\equiv\gamma^{\prime},x:A(\gamma^{\prime})
and
\delta\equiv\delta^{\prime},\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime})
-

then by inductive hypothesis there is a canonical context homotopy equivalence
\langle\;\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime}\;|\;\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}\;\rangle
such that
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma^{\prime})=\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime})
and equivalently
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime}:\Delta^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\alpha(\delta^{\prime}):\textnormal{{g}}(\delta^{\prime})=\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime})
. Moreover, by the following Lemma
4.1.5
there is a canonical homotopy equivalence
\langle\;g^{\prime}\;|\;f^{\prime}\;\rangle
:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime}:\Delta^{\prime},\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime},\underline{x}):A(\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime}))

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime}:\Delta^{\prime},x^{\prime}:A(\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime}))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime},x^{\prime}):A^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime})

relative to
(\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime};\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime})
such that:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime}:\Delta^{\prime},\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime})\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\alpha_{1}(\delta^{\prime},\underline{x}):g(\textnormal{{g}}(\delta^{\prime}),\textnormal{{q}}(\delta^{\prime})^{*}\underline{x})=\alpha(\delta^{\prime})^{*}g^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime},\underline{x})

( i.e.
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime}:\Delta^{\prime},x^{\prime}:A(\textnormal{{g}}(\delta^{\prime}))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\textnormal{{g}}(\delta^{\prime}),\alpha(\delta^{\prime})^{*}x^{\prime})=\textnormal{{q}}(\delta^{\prime})^{*}f^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime},x^{\prime})
)

hence by
(2)
the extension
(\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime},\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime}_{n+1};\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime},\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}_{m+1})
of
\langle\;\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime}\;|\;\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}\;\rangle
via
\langle\;g^{\prime}\;|\;f^{\prime}\;\rangle
(Lemma
3.1.1
) is a canonical context homotopy equivalence
\langle\;\textnormal{{d}}^{\prime}\;|\;\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}\;\rangle
between
\delta^{\prime},\underline{x}\equiv\delta
and
\gamma^{\prime},x\equiv\gamma
and:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime},\underline{x}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;(\alpha(\delta^{\prime}),\alpha_{1}(\delta^{\prime},\underline{x})):(\textnormal{{g}},\textnormal{{g}}_{n+1})(\delta^{\prime},\underline{x})=(\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime},\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime}_{n+1})(\delta^{\prime},\underline{x})

that is:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{d}}(\delta)\equiv(\textnormal{{g}},\textnormal{{g}}_{n+1})(\delta)=(\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime},\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime}_{n+1})(\delta)\equiv\textnormal{{d}}^{\prime}(\delta)

hence we are done. ∎

Lemma 4.1.5
.

Suppose that we are given a canonical homotopy equivalence
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
between
\gamma:\Gamma
and
\delta:\Delta
and suppose that there is a canonical homotopy equivalence
(\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime};\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime})
between
\delta
and
\gamma
such that
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma)=\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}(\gamma)
and equivalently
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\alpha(\delta):\textnormal{{g}}(\delta)=\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime}(\delta)
.

If we are given judgements:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;S(\gamma):\textsc{Type}
and
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta:\Delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;T(\delta):\textsc{Type}

and if there is a canonical homotopy equivalence
\langle\;\varphi\;|\;\psi\;\rangle
:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:S(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\varphi(\gamma,x):T(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}:T(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\psi(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}):S(\gamma)

between
S(\gamma)
and
T(\delta)
relative to a context homotopy equivalence
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
, then there is a canonical homotopy equivalence
\langle\;\psi^{\prime}\;|\;\varphi^{\prime}\;\rangle
:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta:\Delta,\underline{x}:T(\delta)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\psi^{\prime}(\delta,\underline{x}):S(\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime}(\delta))

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta:\Delta,x^{\prime}:S(\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime}(\delta))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\varphi^{\prime}(\delta,x^{\prime}):T(\delta)

relative to
(\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime};\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime})
such that:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta:\Delta,\underline{x}:T(\delta)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\psi(\textnormal{{g}}(\delta),\textnormal{{q}}(\delta)^{*}\underline{x})=\alpha(\delta)^{*}\psi^{\prime}(\delta,\underline{x})

and equivalently
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta:\Delta,x^{\prime}:S(\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime}(\delta))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\varphi(\textnormal{{g}}(\delta),\alpha(\delta)^{*}x^{\prime})=\textnormal{{q}}(\delta)^{*}\varphi^{\prime}(\delta,x^{\prime})
.

Sketch of proof. See
Appendix

A
for more details.

By induction on the complexity of
\langle\;\varphi\;|\;\psi\;\rangle
.

If
\langle\;\varphi\;|\;\psi\;\rangle
is of the form
(a)
then we are done.

If
\langle\;\varphi\;|\;\psi\;\rangle
is of the form
(b)
or
(c)
then either
S(\gamma)\equiv\Pi_{x:A(\gamma)}B(\gamma,x)
and
T(\delta)\equiv\Pi_{\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta)}B^{\prime}(\delta,\underline{x})
or
S(\gamma)\equiv\Sigma_{x:A(\gamma)}B(\gamma,x)
and
T(\delta)\equiv\Sigma_{\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta)}B^{\prime}(\delta,\underline{x})
for some judgements:

\succ

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}
and
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;B(\gamma,x):\textsc{Type}

\succ

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta:\Delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A^{\prime}(\delta):\textsc{Type}
and
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta:\Delta,\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;B^{\prime}(\delta,\underline{x}):\textsc{Type}

and there are a canonical homotopy equivalence
\langle\;f_{1}\;|\;g_{1}\;\rangle
between
A(\gamma)
and
A^{\prime}(\delta)
relative to
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
and a canonical homotopy equivalence
\langle\;f_{2}\;|\;g_{2}\;\rangle
between
B(\gamma,x)
and
B^{\prime}(\delta,\underline{x})
relative to the extension
(\textnormal{{f}},\textnormal{{f}}_{m+1};\textnormal{{g}},\textnormal{{g}}_{n+1})
of
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
via
(f_{1};g_{1})
(Lemma
3.1.1
), in such a way that
\langle\;\varphi\;|\;\psi\;\rangle
is either the homotopy equivalence
(f^{\Pi};g^{\Pi})
of Lemma
3.2.1
or
(f^{\Sigma};g^{\Sigma})
of Lemma
3.3.1
. By inductive hypothesis there are a canonical homotopy equivalence
\langle\;g^{\prime}_{1}\;|\;f^{\prime}_{1}\;\rangle
between
A^{\prime}(\delta)
and
A(\gamma)
relative to
(\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime};\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime})
and a canonical homotopy equivalence
\langle\;g^{\prime}_{2}\;|\;f^{\prime}_{2}\;\rangle
between
B^{\prime}(\delta,\underline{x})
and
B(\gamma,x)
relative to the extension
(\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime},\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime}_{n+1};\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime},\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}_{m+1})
of
(\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime};\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime})
via
(g^{\prime}_{1};f^{\prime}_{1})
, in such a way that:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta:\Delta,\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\alpha_{1}(\delta,\underline{x}):g_{1}(\textnormal{{g}}(\delta),\textnormal{{q}}(\delta)^{*}\underline{x})=\alpha(\delta)^{*}g^{\prime}_{1}(\delta,\underline{x})

i.e.
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta:\Delta,x^{\prime}:A(\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime}(\delta))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f_{1}(\textnormal{{g}}(\delta),\alpha(\delta)^{*}x^{\prime})=\textnormal{{q}}(\delta)^{*}f^{\prime}_{1}(\delta,x^{\prime})

and that:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta:\Delta,\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta),\underline{y}:B^{\prime}(\delta,\underline{x})\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g_{2}(\textnormal{{g}}(\delta),\textnormal{{g}}_{n+1}(\delta,\underline{x}),(\textnormal{{q}}(\delta),\textnormal{{q}}_{n+1}(\delta,\underline{x}))^{*}\underline{y})=(\alpha(\delta),\alpha_{1}(\delta,\underline{x}))^{*}g^{\prime}_{1}(\delta,\underline{x},\underline{y})

i.e.
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta:\Delta,\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta),y^{\prime}:B(\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime}(\delta),g_{1}^{\prime}(\delta,\underline{x}))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f_{2}(\textnormal{{g}}(\delta),\textnormal{{g}}_{n+1}(\delta,\underline{x}),(\alpha(\delta),\alpha_{1}(\delta,\underline{x}))^{*}y^{\prime})=(\textnormal{{q}}(\delta),\textnormal{{q}}_{n+1}(\delta,\underline{x}))^{*}f^{\prime}_{2}(\delta,\underline{x},y^{\prime})

where we remind that:

\displaystyle\textnormal{{g}}_{n+1}(\delta,\underline{x})

\displaystyle\equiv g_{1}(\textnormal{{g}}(\delta),\textnormal{{q}}(\delta)^{*}\underline{x})

\displaystyle\textnormal{{q}}_{n+1}(\delta,\underline{x})

\displaystyle\equiv q_{1}(\textnormal{{g}}(\delta),\textnormal{{q}}(\delta)^{*}\underline{x})^{-1}

in context
\delta,\underline{x}
, being
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}:A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;q_{1}(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}):\underline{x}^{\prime}=f_{1}(\gamma,g_{1}(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}))
. Now
\langle\;g^{\prime\Pi}\;|\;f^{\prime\Pi}\;\rangle
is a canonical homotopy equivalence between
\Pi_{\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta)}B^{\prime}(\delta,\underline{x})
and
\Pi_{x:A(\gamma)}B(\gamma,x)
and relative to
(\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime};\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime})
, by
(b)
, and
\langle\;g^{\prime\Sigma}\;|\;f^{\prime\Sigma}\;\rangle
is a canonical homotopy equivalence between
\Sigma_{\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta)}B^{\prime}(\delta,\underline{x})
and
\Sigma_{x:A(\gamma)}B(\gamma,x)
and relative to
(\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime};\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime})
, by
(c)
. One is therefore left to verify that:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta,z^{\prime}:\Pi_{x^{\prime}:A(g^{\prime}(\delta))}B(\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime}(\delta),x^{\prime})\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f^{\Pi}(\textnormal{{g}}(\delta),\alpha(\delta)^{*}z^{\prime})=\textnormal{{q}}(\delta)^{*}f^{\prime\Pi}(\delta,z^{\prime})

and that:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta,\underline{u}:\Sigma_{x^{\prime}:A^{\prime}(\delta)}B^{\prime}(\delta,x^{\prime})\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g^{\Sigma}(\textnormal{{g}}(\delta),\textnormal{{q}}(\delta)^{*}\underline{u})=\alpha(\delta)^{*}g^{\prime\Sigma}(\delta,\underline{u}).

If
\langle\;\varphi\;|\;\psi\;\rangle
is of the form
(d)
one reasons similarly.∎

Lemma 4.1.6
.

Let
\gamma:\Gamma
,
\delta:\Delta
and
\omega:\Omega
be contexts, let
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
be a canonical context homotopy equivalence between
\gamma
and
\delta
and let
(\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime},\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime})
be a context homotopy equivalence between
\delta
and
\omega
.

If we are given judgements
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;S(\gamma):\textsc{Type}
,
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;T(\delta):\textsc{Type}
and
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \omega\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;U(\omega):\textsc{Type}
and canonical homotopy equivalences
\langle\;\varphi\;|\;\psi\;\rangle
between
S(\gamma)
and
T(\delta)
relative to
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
and
\langle\;\varphi^{\prime}\;|\;\psi^{\prime}\;\rangle
between
T(\delta)
and
U(\omega)
relative to
(\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime};\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime})
, then there exists a canonical homotopy equivalence
\langle\;{\varphi}^{\prime\prime}\;|\;{\psi}^{\prime\prime}\;\rangle
between
S(\gamma)
and
U(\omega)
relative to
(\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}}\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime})
and such that:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,s:S(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;{\varphi}^{\prime\prime}(\gamma,s)=\varphi^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma),\varphi(\gamma,s))

and equivalently
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,u^{\prime\prime}:U(\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma)))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;{\psi}^{\prime\prime}(\gamma,u^{\prime\prime})=\psi(\gamma,\psi^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma),u^{\prime\prime}))
.

Proof.

By induction on the complexity of
\langle\;\varphi\;|\;\psi\;\rangle
.
∎

Lemma 4.1.7
.

Let
\gamma:\Gamma
and
\delta:\Delta
be contexts and let
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
and
(\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime};\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime})
be context homotopy equivalences between
\gamma
and
\delta
. Let us assume that
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\alpha(\gamma):\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma)=\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}(\gamma)
and equivalently
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{g}}(\delta)=\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime}(\delta)
.

If we are given judgements
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;S(\gamma):\textsc{Type}
and
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;T(\delta):\textsc{Type}
and a canonical homotopy equivalence
\langle\;\varphi\;|\;\psi\;\rangle
between
S(\gamma)
and
T(\delta)
relative to
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
, then there is a canonical homotopy equivalence
\langle\;\varphi^{\prime}\;|\;\psi^{\prime}\;\rangle
between
S(\gamma)
and
T(\delta)
relative to
(\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime};\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime})
such that:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,s:S(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\varphi(\gamma,s)=\alpha(\gamma)^{*}\varphi^{\prime}(\gamma,s)

and equivalently
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,t^{\prime}:T(\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}(\gamma))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;{\psi}(\gamma,\alpha(\gamma)^{*}t^{\prime})=\psi^{\prime}(\gamma,t^{\prime})
.

Proof.

By induction on the complexity of
\langle\;\varphi\;|\;\psi\;\rangle
.
∎

Proposition 4.1.8

(Transitivity)
.

Let
\gamma:\Gamma
,
\delta:\Delta
and
\omega:\Omega
be contexts. If
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
is a canonical context homotopy equivalence between
\gamma
and
\delta
and
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}^{\prime}\;\rangle
is a canonical context homotopy equivalence between
\delta
and
\omega
then there is a canonical context homotopy equivalence
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime\prime}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}^{\prime\prime}\;\rangle
between
\gamma
and
\omega
such that
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime\prime}(\gamma)=\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}(\textnormal{{c}}(\gamma))
and equivalently
