{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{d}}^{\prime\prime}(\delta)=\textnormal{{d}}(\textnormal{{d}}^{\prime}(\delta))
.

Proof.

By induction on the complexity of
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
. If
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
is of the form
(a)
then
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}^{\prime}\;\rangle
is of the form
(a)
as well and we are done. Let
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
be of the form
(b)
, hence
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}^{\prime}\;\rangle
is of the form
(b)
as well. Then
\gamma:\Gamma
,
\delta:\Delta
and
\omega:\Omega
are of the form
\gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime},x:A(\gamma^{\prime})
and
\delta^{\prime}:\Delta^{\prime},\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime})
and
\omega^{\prime}:\Omega^{\prime},\underline{\underline{x}}:A^{\prime\prime}(\Omega^{\prime})
respectively. Moreover there are a canonical context homotopy equivalence:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma^{\prime}):\Delta^{\prime}

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime}:\Delta^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{g}}(\delta^{\prime}):\Gamma^{\prime}

and a canonical homotopy equivalence:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime},x:A(\gamma^{\prime})\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\gamma^{\prime},x):A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma^{\prime}))

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime},\underline{x}^{\prime}:A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma^{\prime}))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g(\gamma^{\prime},\underline{x}^{\prime}):A(\gamma^{\prime})

between
A(\gamma^{\prime})
and
A^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime})
relative to
\langle\;\textnormal{{f}}\;|\;\textnormal{{g}}\;\rangle
in such a way that the extension:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime},x:A(\gamma^{\prime})\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;(\textnormal{{f}},\textnormal{{f}}_{m+1})(\gamma^{\prime},x):\Delta^{\prime},A^{\prime}

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime}:\Delta^{\prime},\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime})\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;(\textnormal{{g}},\textnormal{{g}}_{n+1})(\delta^{\prime},\underline{x}):\Gamma^{\prime},A

of
\langle\;\textnormal{{f}}\;|\;\textnormal{{g}}\;\rangle
via
\langle\;f\;|\;g\;\rangle
(Lemma
3.1.1
) is the given
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
. Analogously, there are a canonical context homotopy equivalence:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime}:\Delta^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime}):\Omega^{\prime}

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \omega^{\prime}:\Omega^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{g}}(\omega^{\prime}):\Delta^{\prime}

and a canonical homotopy equivalence:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime}:\Delta^{\prime},\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime})\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime},\underline{x}):A^{\prime\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime}))

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime}:\Delta^{\prime},\underline{\underline{x}}:A^{\prime\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime}))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime},\underline{\underline{x}}^{\prime}):A^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime})

between
A^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime})
and
A^{\prime\prime}(\omega^{\prime})
relative to
\langle\;\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}\;|\;\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime}\;\rangle
in such a way that the extension:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime}:\Delta^{\prime},\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime})\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;(\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime},\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}_{m+1})(\delta^{\prime},\underline{x}):\Omega^{\prime},A^{\prime\prime}

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \omega^{\prime}:\Omega^{\prime},\underline{\underline{x}}:A^{\prime\prime}(\omega^{\prime})\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;(\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime},\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime}_{n+1})(\omega^{\prime},\underline{\underline{x}}):\Delta^{\prime},A^{\prime}

of
\langle\;\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}\;|\;\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime}\;\rangle
via
\langle\;f^{\prime}\;|\;g^{\prime}\;\rangle
(Lemma
3.1.1
) is the given
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}^{\prime}\;\rangle
. By inductive hypothesis there is a canonical context homotopy equivalence
\langle\;\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime\prime}\;|\;\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime\prime}\;\rangle
between
\gamma^{\prime}
and
\omega^{\prime}
such that:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime\prime}(\gamma^{\prime})=\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma^{\prime}))
and
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \omega^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime\prime}(\omega^{\prime})=\textnormal{{g}}(\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime}(\omega^{\prime}))

Let us consider the canonical homotopy equivalence:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime},x:A(\gamma^{\prime})\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\tilde{f}(\gamma^{\prime},x)\equiv f^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma^{\prime}),f(\gamma^{\prime},x)):A^{\prime\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma^{\prime})))

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime},\underline{\underline{x}}^{\prime\prime}:A^{\prime\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma^{\prime})))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\tilde{g}(\gamma^{\prime},\underline{\underline{x}}^{\prime\prime})\equiv g(\gamma^{\prime},g^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma^{\prime}),\underline{\underline{x}}^{\prime\prime})):A(\gamma^{\prime})

relative to
(\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}}\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime})
and let us observe that:

\displaystyle(\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma)),f^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma^{\prime}),f(\gamma^{\prime},x)))

\displaystyle\equiv(\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma)),\textnormal{{f}}_{m+1}^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma^{\prime}),\textnormal{{f}}_{m+1}(\gamma^{\prime},x)))

\displaystyle\equiv(\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime},\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}_{m+1})(\textnormal{{f}},\textnormal{{f}}_{m+1})(\gamma^{\prime},x)

\displaystyle\equiv\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}(\textnormal{{c}}(\gamma))

hence the extension of
(\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}}\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime})
via
(\tilde{f};\tilde{g})
is pairwise propositionally equal to
(\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}\textnormal{{c}};\textnormal{{d}}\textnormal{{d}}^{\prime})
. By Lemma
4.1.6
, there is canonical homotopy equivalence
\langle\;\overline{f}\;|\;\overline{g}\;\rangle
between
A(\gamma^{\prime})
and
A^{\prime\prime}(\omega^{\prime})
relative to
(\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}}\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime})
such that
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\overline{f}(\gamma^{\prime})=\tilde{f}(\gamma^{\prime})
and equivalently
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \omega^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\overline{g}(\omega^{\prime})=\tilde{g}(\omega^{\prime})
. Then, again, the extension of
(\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}}\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime})
via
\langle\;\overline{f}\;|\;\overline{g}\;\rangle
is pairwise propositionally equal to
(\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}\textnormal{{c}};\textnormal{{d}}\textnormal{{d}}^{\prime})
. Since
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\alpha(\gamma^{\prime}):\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma^{\prime}))=\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime\prime}(\gamma^{\prime})
and equivalently
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \omega^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{g}}(\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime}(\omega^{\prime}))=\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime\prime}(\omega^{\prime})
, by Lemma
4.1.7
there is a canonical homotopy equivalence
\langle\;f^{\prime\prime}\;|\;g^{\prime\prime}\;\rangle
between
A(\gamma^{\prime})
and
A^{\prime\prime}(\omega^{\prime})
relative to
\langle\;\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime\prime}\;|\;\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime\prime}\;\rangle
and such that:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime},x:A(\gamma^{\prime})\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\overline{f}(\gamma^{\prime},x)=\alpha(\gamma^{\prime})^{*}f^{\prime\prime}(\gamma^{\prime},x)

and equivalently
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime},\underline{\underline{x}}^{\prime}:A^{\prime\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime\prime}(\gamma^{\prime}))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\overline{g}(\gamma^{\prime},\alpha(\gamma^{\prime})^{*}\underline{\underline{x}}^{\prime})=g^{\prime\prime}(\gamma^{\prime},\underline{\underline{x}}^{\prime}).

Therefore the extension
\langle\;\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime\prime},\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime\prime}_{m+1}\;|\;\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime\prime},\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime\prime}_{n+1}\;\rangle
of
\langle\;\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime\prime}\;|\;\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime\prime}\;\rangle
via
\langle\;f^{\prime\prime}\;|\;g^{\prime\prime}\;\rangle
is a canonical context homotopy equivalence by
(b)
and
\langle\;\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime\prime},\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime\prime}_{m+1}\;|\;\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime\prime},\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime\prime}_{n+1}\;\rangle
is pairwise propositionally equal to the extension of
(\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}}\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime})
via
\langle\;\overline{f}\;|\;\overline{g}\;\rangle
. In particular
\langle\;\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime\prime},\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime\prime}_{m+1}\;|\;\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime\prime},\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime\prime}_{n+1}\;\rangle
is a canonical context homotopy equivalence between
\gamma
and
\delta
that is pairwise propositionally equal to
(\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}\textnormal{{c}};\textnormal{{d}}\textnormal{{d}}^{\prime})
. ∎

We conclude the current subsection with a brief list of technical results that we are using in
Section

5
and
Section

6
.

Lemma 4.1.9
.

Let
\gamma:\Gamma
,
\gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime}
and
\delta:\Delta
be contexts, let
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
be a context homotopy equivalence between
\gamma
and
\gamma^{\prime}
and let
a(\gamma),a^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime}):\Delta
be context morphisms
\gamma\to\delta
and
\gamma^{\prime}\to\delta
respectively such that:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\gamma^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a(\gamma)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha(\gamma)}

Then, whenever
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;S(\delta):\textsc{Type}
, there is a canonical homotopy equivalence
\langle\;\varphi\;|\;\psi\;\rangle
between
S(a(\gamma))
and
S(a^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime}))
relative to
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
such that:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x:S(a(\gamma))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\alpha(\gamma)^{*}\varphi(\gamma,x)=x
and equivalently
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}:S(a^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma)))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\psi(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime})=\alpha(\gamma)^{*}\underline{x}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

By induction on the complexity of
S(\delta)
.
∎

Corollary 4.1.10
.

Let
\gamma:\Gamma
and
\gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime}
be contexts and let
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
be a context homotopy equivalence between
\gamma
and
\gamma^{\prime}
. Then, whenever
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;S(\gamma^{\prime}):\textsc{Type}
, there is a canonical homotopy equivalence
\langle\;\varphi\;|\;\psi\;\rangle
between
S(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))
and
S(\gamma^{\prime})
relative to
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
and such that:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x:S(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\varphi(\gamma,x)=\psi(\gamma,x)=x.

Proof.

Follows by Lemma
4.1.9
, where
\delta\equiv\gamma^{\prime}
,
a(\gamma)\equiv\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma)
,
a^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime})\equiv\gamma^{\prime}
and
\alpha(\gamma)\equiv\textnormal{{r}}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))
.
∎

Lemma 4.1.11
.

Let
\gamma:\Gamma
and
\gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime}
be contexts and let
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
be a context homotopy equivalence between
\gamma
and
\gamma^{\prime}
. Suppose that
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;S(\gamma):\textsc{Type}
and
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;T(\gamma^{\prime}):\textsc{Type}
are canonically homotopy equivalent via some
\langle\;\varphi\;|\;\psi\;\rangle
relative to
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
. Let
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
be some canonical context homotopy equivalence between
\gamma
and itself such that
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
is pairwise propositionally equal to
({\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma;{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma)
(whose existence is ensured by Proposition
4.1.2
) and let
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\alpha(\gamma):\textnormal{{c}}(\gamma)=\gamma
.

Then
S(\gamma)
and
T(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))
are canonically homotopy equivalent via some
\langle\;\varphi^{\prime}\;|\;\psi^{\prime}\;\rangle
relative to
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
in such a way that:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x:S(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\varphi^{\prime}(\gamma,x)=\alpha(\gamma)^{*}\varphi(\gamma,x)

and equivalently
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}:T(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\psi^{\prime}(\gamma,\alpha(\gamma)^{*}\underline{x}^{\prime})=\psi(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime})
.

Proof.

By induction on the complexity of
\langle\;\varphi\;|\;\psi\;\rangle
.
∎

Lemma 4.1.12
.

Let
\gamma:\Gamma
and
\delta:\Delta
be contexts, let
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
be a canonical homotopy equivalence between
\delta
and itself such that
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\alpha(\delta):\textnormal{{f}}(\delta)=\delta
and let
(\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime};\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime})
be a canonical homotopy equivalence between
\gamma
and itself such that
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\alpha^{\prime}(\gamma):\gamma=\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}(\gamma)
. Moreover, let
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;a(\gamma):\Delta
be a context morphism and let
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;S(\delta):\textsc{Type}
and
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;T(\delta):\textsc{Type}
be such that there is a canonical homotopy equivalence
\langle\;\varphi\;|\;\psi\;\rangle
between
S(\delta)
and
T(\delta)
relative to
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
. Then there is a canonical homotopy equivalence
\langle\;\varphi^{\prime}\;|\;\psi^{\prime}\;\rangle
between
S(a(\gamma))
and
T(a(\gamma))
relative to
(\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime};\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime})
and such that:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x:S(a(\gamma))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\varphi(a(\gamma),x)=\alpha^{\prime\prime}(\gamma)^{*}\varphi^{\prime}(\gamma,x)

i.e.
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,\underline{x}:T(a(\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}(\gamma)))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\psi(a(\gamma),\alpha^{\prime\prime}(\gamma)^{*}\underline{x})=\psi^{\prime}(\gamma,\underline{x})
where
\alpha^{\prime\prime}(\gamma)\equiv\alpha(a(\gamma))\bullet a(\alpha^{\prime}(\gamma))
.

Proof.

By induction on the complexity of
\langle\;\varphi\;|\;\psi\;\rangle
.
∎

4.2
Properties of a restriction of the family

In this subsection we restrict the family of the canonical context homotopy equivalences and prove additional properties satisfied by this restriction. Again, we remind that we use the notation
(\;\cdot\;;\;\cdot\;)
to indicate any homotopy equivalence between contexts or types in context, reserving the symbol
\langle\;\cdot\;|\;\cdot\;\rangle
for the canonical ones. Moreover, we remind that a type judgement
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;T(\delta):\textsc{Type}
is said to be an
h-proposition
if
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta,x_{1},x_{2}:T(\delta)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\alpha(\delta,x_{1},x_{2}):x_{1}=x_{2}
. We start by giving the following inductive definition:

Definition 4.2.1
.

A type judgement
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;T(\delta):\textsc{Type}
in some context
\delta:\Delta

has h-propositional identities
(or is
with h-propositional identities
) if it belongs to the smallest family
\mathcal{F}
of type judgements that satisfies the following clauses:

\succ

a judgement
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;S:\textsc{Type}
(where
S
is a atomic type) belongs to
\mathcal{F}
;

\succ

a judgement
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\Pi_{x:A(\gamma)}B(\gamma,x):\textsc{Type}
, for some
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}
of
\mathcal{F}
and some
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;B(\gamma,x):\textsc{Type}
of
\mathcal{F}
, belongs to
\mathcal{F}
;

\succ

a judgement
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\Sigma_{x:A(\gamma)}B(\gamma,x):\textsc{Type}
, for some
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}
of
\mathcal{F}
and some
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;B(\gamma,x):\textsc{Type}
of
\mathcal{F}
, belongs to
\mathcal{F}
;

\succ

an h-proposition
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;s_{1}(\gamma)=s_{2}(\gamma):\textsc{Type}
, for some
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}
of
\mathcal{F}
and some
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;s_{1}(\gamma),s_{2}(\gamma):A(\gamma)
, belongs to
\mathcal{F}
.

Despite our choice of terminology, we would like to clarify that the notion of a type with h-propositional identities does not refer to the types
T
whose identity types
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ x,y:T\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;x=y:\textsc{Type}
are h-propositions, i.e. the
h-sets
. Rather, it characterises types whose equalities
involved in their construction
are h-propositions.

Definition 4.2.2
.

Let
\gamma:\Gamma
be a context
\gamma_{1}:\Gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}:\Gamma_{2}(\gamma_{1}),...,\gamma_{n}:\Gamma_{n}(\gamma_{1},...,\gamma_{n-1})
, where
n
might be
0
. For any
i\in\{1,...,n\}
, let
\gamma^{i}
be the context
\gamma_{1},...,\gamma_{i}
. We say that
\gamma

has h-propositional identities
(or is
with h-propositional identities
) if, for every
i\in\{1,...,n\}
, the judgement
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{i}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\Gamma_{i}(\gamma^{i})
has h-propositional identities.

Lemma 4.2.3
.

Let
\gamma:\Gamma
be a context
\gamma_{1}:\Gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}:\Gamma_{2}(\gamma_{1}),...,\gamma_{n}:\Gamma_{n}(\gamma_{1},...,\gamma_{n-1})
and let
\delta:\Delta
be a context
\delta_{1}:\Delta_{1},\delta_{2}:\Delta_{2}(\delta_{1}),...,\delta_{m}:\Delta_{m}(\delta_{1},...,\delta_{m-1})
. If there is a canonical context homotopy equivalence
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
between
\gamma:\Gamma
and
\delta:\Delta
then
\gamma
has h-propositional identities if and only if
\delta
has h-propositional identities.

Proof.

By Proposition
4.1.4
we can assume w.l.o.g. that
\gamma
has h-propositional identities and we are left to verify that
\delta
has h-propositional identities as well. By induction on the complexity of
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
. If
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
is of the form
(1)
then we are done. If
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
is of the form
(2)
then
\gamma:\Gamma
and
\delta:\Delta
are of the form
\gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime},x:A(\gamma^{\prime})
and
\delta^{\prime}:\Delta^{\prime},\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime})
respectively and there are a canonical context homotopy equivalence
\langle\;\textnormal{{f}}\;|\;\textnormal{{g}}\;\rangle
:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma^{\prime}):\Delta^{\prime}

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime}:\Delta^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{g}}(\delta^{\prime}):\Gamma^{\prime}

and a canonical homotopy equivalence
\langle\;f\;|\;g\;\rangle
:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime},x:A(\gamma^{\prime})\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\gamma^{\prime},x):A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma^{\prime}))

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime},\underline{x}^{\prime}:A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma^{\prime}))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g(\gamma^{\prime},\underline{x}^{\prime}):A(\gamma^{\prime})

between
A(\gamma^{\prime})
and
A^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime})
relative to
\langle\;\textnormal{{f}}\;|\;\textnormal{{g}}\;\rangle
, in such a way that the extension:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime},x:A(\gamma^{\prime})\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;(\textnormal{{f}},\textnormal{{f}}_{m+1})(\gamma^{\prime},x):\Delta^{\prime},A^{\prime}

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime}:\Delta^{\prime},\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime})\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;(\textnormal{{g}},\textnormal{{g}}_{n+1})(\delta^{\prime},\underline{x}):\Gamma^{\prime},A

of
\langle\;\textnormal{{f}}\;|\;\textnormal{{g}}\;\rangle
via
\langle\;f\;|\;g\;\rangle
(Lemma
3.1.1
) is the canonical context homotopy equivalence
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
. By inductive hypothesis and being
\gamma^{\prime}
a context with h-propositional identities, the context
\delta^{\prime}
has h-propositional identities. Moreover, by the following Lemma
4.2.4
and having
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma^{\prime}):\textsc{Type}
h-propositional identities, we deduce that
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime}):\textsc{Type}
has h-propositional identities. Therefore
\delta
has h-propositional identities and we are done.
∎

Lemma 4.2.4
.

Let
\gamma:\Gamma
and
\delta:\Delta
be contexts and let
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
be a canonical context homotopy equivalence between
\gamma
and
\delta
. Let
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;S(\gamma):\textsc{Type}
and
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;T(\delta):\textsc{Type}
be such that there is a canonical homotopy equivalence
\langle\;\varphi\;|\;\psi\;\rangle
between
S(\gamma)
and
T(\delta)
relative to
\langle\;\textnormal{{f}}\;|\;\textnormal{{g}}\;\rangle
. Then
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;S(\gamma):\textsc{Type}
has h-propositional identities if and only if
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;T(\delta):\textsc{Type}
has h-propositional identities.

Proof.

By induction on the complexity of
\langle\;\varphi\;|\;\psi\;\rangle
. If
\langle\;\varphi\;|\;\psi\;\rangle
is of the form
(a)
then we are done. If
\langle\;\varphi\;|\;\psi\;\rangle
is of the form
(b)
then
S(\gamma)
is of the form
\Pi_{x:A(\gamma)}B(\gamma,x)
and
T(\delta)
is of the form
\Pi_{x^{\prime}:A^{\prime}(\delta)}B(\delta,x^{\prime})
, where
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}
and
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;B(\gamma,x):\textsc{Type}
have h-propositional identities,
A(\gamma)
is canonically homotopy equivalent to
A^{\prime}(\delta)
relative to
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
and
B(\gamma,x)
is canonically homotopy equivalent to
B^{\prime}(\delta,x^{\prime})
relative to the extension of
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
via the given canonical homotopy equivalence between
A(\gamma)
and
A^{\prime}(\delta)
relative to
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
. By inductive hypothesis, the judgements
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A^{\prime}(\delta):\textsc{Type}
and
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta,x^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;B^{\prime}(\delta,x^{\prime}):\textsc{Type}
have h-propositional identities, hence
T(\delta)
has h-propositional identities and we are done. If
\langle\;\varphi\;|\;\psi\;\rangle
is of the form
(c)
then we infer that
T(\delta)
has h-propositional identities as for the case
(b)
. Finally, if
\langle\;\varphi\;|\;\psi\;\rangle
is of the form
(d)
then
S(\gamma)
is an h-proposition of the form
s_{1}(\gamma)=s_{2}(\gamma)
for some
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}
with h-propositional identities and some
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;s_{1}(\gamma),s_{2}(\gamma):A(\gamma)
, while
T(\delta)
is of the form
t_{1}(\delta)=t_{2}(\delta)
for some
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A^{\prime}(\delta):\textsc{Type}
and some
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;t_{1}(\delta),t_{2}(\delta):A^{\prime}(\delta)
, being
A(\gamma)
and
A^{\prime}(\delta)
canonically homotopy equivalent relative to
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
in such a way that the canonical homotopy equivalence propositionally identifies
s_{i}(\gamma)
and
t_{i}(\gamma)
, for
i\in\{1,2\}
. By inductive hypothesis, the judgement
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A^{\prime}(\delta):\textsc{Type}
has h-propositional identities. Moreover
t_{1}(\delta)=t_{2}(\delta)
happens to be an h-proposition, since it is homotopy equivalent (via
\langle\;\varphi\;|\;\psi\;\rangle
) to an h-set. Hence
T(\delta)
has h-propositional identities and we are done.
∎

Proposition 4.2.5
.

Let
\gamma:\Gamma
and
\delta:\Delta
be contexts with h-propositional identities. If
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
and
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}^{\prime}\;\rangle
are canonical context homotopy equivalences between
\gamma:\Gamma
and
\delta:\Delta
, then
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{c}}(\gamma)=\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}(\gamma)
(i.e.
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta:\Delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{d}}(\delta)=\textnormal{{d}}^{\prime}(\delta)
).

Proof.

By induction on the complexity of
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
. If
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
is of the form
(1)
then
\gamma:\Gamma
and
\delta:\Delta
are the empty contexts hence
\textnormal{{c}}\equiv\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}
and
\textnormal{{d}}\equiv\textnormal{{d}}^{\prime}
. If
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
is of the form
(2)
then
\gamma:\Gamma
and
\delta:\Delta
are of the form
\gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime},x:A(\gamma^{\prime})
and
\delta^{\prime}:\Delta^{\prime},\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime})
respectively and there are a canonical context homotopy equivalence
\langle\;\textnormal{{f}}\;|\;\textnormal{{g}}\;\rangle
:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma^{\prime}):\Delta^{\prime}

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime}:\Delta^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{g}}(\delta^{\prime}):\Gamma^{\prime}

and a canonical homotopy equivalence
\langle\;f\;|\;g\;\rangle
:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime},x:A(\gamma^{\prime})\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\gamma^{\prime},x):A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma^{\prime}))

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime},\underline{x}^{\prime}:A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma^{\prime}))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g(\gamma^{\prime},\underline{x}^{\prime}):A(\gamma^{\prime})

between
A(\gamma^{\prime})
and
A^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime})
relative to
\langle\;\textnormal{{f}}\;|\;\textnormal{{g}}\;\rangle
, in such a way that the extension:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime},x:A(\gamma^{\prime})\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;(\textnormal{{f}},\textnormal{{f}}_{n+1})(\gamma^{\prime},x):\Delta^{\prime},A^{\prime}

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime}:\Delta^{\prime},\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime})\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;(\textnormal{{g}},\textnormal{{g}}_{n+1})(\delta^{\prime},\underline{x}):\Gamma^{\prime},A

of
\langle\;\textnormal{{f}}\;|\;\textnormal{{g}}\;\rangle
via
\langle\;f\;|\;g\;\rangle
(Lemma
3.1.1
) is the given
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
. Therefore
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}^{\prime}\;\rangle
cannot be of the form
(1)
as (e.g.)
\delta:\Delta
is not the empty context, which means that
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}^{\prime}\;\rangle
, being canonical, is of the form
(2)
. Hence, as before, there are a canonical context homotopy equivalence
\langle\;\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}\;|\;\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime}\;\rangle
:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime}):\Delta^{\prime}

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime}:\Delta^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime}):\Gamma^{\prime}

and a canonical homotopy equivalence
\langle\;f^{\prime}\;|\;g^{\prime}\;\rangle
:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime},x:A(\gamma^{\prime})\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime},x):A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime}))

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime},\underline{\underline{x}}^{\prime}:A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime}))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime},\underline{\underline{x}}^{\prime}):A(\gamma^{\prime})

between
A(\gamma^{\prime})
and
A^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime})
relative to
\langle\;\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}\;|\;\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime}\;\rangle
, in such a way that the extension:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime},x:A(\gamma^{\prime})\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;(\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime},\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}_{n+1})(\gamma^{\prime},x):\Delta^{\prime},A^{\prime}

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime}:\Delta^{\prime},\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime})\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;(\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime},\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime}_{n+1})(\delta^{\prime},\underline{x}):\Gamma^{\prime},A

of
\langle\;\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}\;|\;\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime}\;\rangle
via
\langle\;f^{\prime}\;|\;g^{\prime}\;\rangle
(Lemma
3.1.1
) is the given
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}^{\prime}\;\rangle
. Now
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\alpha(\gamma^{\prime}):\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma^{\prime})=\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime})
(i.e.
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime}:\Delta^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\tilde{\alpha}(\delta^{\prime}):\textnormal{{g}}(\delta^{\prime})=\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime})
) by inductive hypothesis.

Let us pretend that we know that:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime},x\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime},x)=\alpha(\gamma^{\prime})^{*}f^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime},x)

( i.e.
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime},\underline{\underline{x}}^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g(\gamma^{\prime},\alpha(\gamma^{\prime})^{*}\underline{\underline{x}}^{\prime})=g^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime},\underline{\underline{x}}^{\prime})
), that is:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime},x\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{f}}_{n+1}(\gamma^{\prime},x)=\alpha(\gamma^{\prime})^{*}\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}_{n+1}(\gamma^{\prime},x)
( i.e.
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime},\underline{x}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{g}}_{n+1}(\delta^{\prime},\underline{x})=\tilde{\alpha}(\delta^{\prime})^{*}\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime}_{n+1}(\delta^{\prime},\underline{x})
).

Then
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime},x\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;(\textnormal{{f}},\textnormal{{f}}_{n+1})(\gamma^{\prime},x)=(\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime},\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}_{n+1})(\gamma^{\prime},x)
( i.e.
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime},\underline{x}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;(\textnormal{{g}},\textnormal{{g}}_{n+1})(\delta^{\prime},\underline{x})=(\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime},\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime}_{n+1})(\delta^{\prime},\underline{x})
) that is
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{c}}(\gamma)=\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}(\gamma)
(i.e.
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta:\Delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{d}}(\delta)=\textnormal{{d}}^{\prime}(\delta)
).

Hence we are done if
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime},x\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime},x)=\alpha(\gamma^{\prime})^{*}f^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime},x)
( i.e.
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime},\underline{\underline{x}}^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g(\gamma^{\prime},\alpha(\gamma^{\prime})^{*}\underline{\underline{x}}^{\prime})=g^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime},\underline{\underline{x}}^{\prime})
), but this is actually the case by the following Lemma
4.2.6
.
∎

Lemma 4.2.6
.

Let
\gamma:\Gamma
and
\delta:\Delta
be contexts together with context homotopy equivalences
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
and
(\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime};\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime})
such that:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\alpha(\gamma):\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma)=\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}(\gamma)
(i.e.
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta:\Delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\tilde{\alpha}(\delta):\textnormal{{g}}(\delta)=\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime}(\delta)
)

and let
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;S(\gamma):\textsc{Type}
and
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta:\Delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;T(\delta):\textsc{Type}
have h-propositional identities. Moreover, let
\langle\;\varphi\;|\;\psi\;\rangle
be a canonical homotopy equivalence between
S(\gamma)
and
T(\delta)
relative to
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
and let
\langle\;\varphi^{\prime}\;|\;\psi^{\prime}\;\rangle
be a canonical homotopy equivalence between
S(\gamma)
and
T(\delta)
relative to
(\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime};\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime})
. Then
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,s:S(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\varphi(\gamma,s)=\alpha(\gamma)^{*}\varphi^{\prime}(\gamma,s)
i.e.
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,\underline{t}^{\prime}:T(\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}(\gamma))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\psi(\gamma,\alpha(\gamma)^{*}\underline{t}^{\prime})=\psi^{\prime}(\gamma,\underline{t}^{\prime})
.

Proof.

By induction on the complexity of
S(\gamma)
.

If
S(\gamma)
is a base type
S
then, being
\langle\;\varphi\;|\;\psi\;\rangle
a canonical homotopy equivalence, the context
\delta
needs to be
\gamma
and
T(\delta)
needs to be
S
, hence both
\langle\;\varphi\;|\;\psi\;\rangle
and
\langle\;\varphi^{\prime}\;|\;\psi^{\prime}\;\rangle
need to be of the form
(a)
and we are done.

If
S(\gamma)
is of the form
\Pi_{x:A(\gamma)}B(\gamma,x)
for some
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}
with h-propositional identities and some
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;B(\gamma,x):\textsc{Type}
with h-propositional identities then, being
\langle\;\varphi\;|\;\psi\;\rangle
a canonical homotopy equivalence, the type
T(\delta)
needs to be of the form
\Pi_{x^{\prime}:A^{\prime}(\delta)}B^{\prime}(\delta,x^{\prime})
for some
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A^{\prime}(\delta):\textsc{Type}
with h-propositional identities and some
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta,x^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;B(\delta,x^{\prime}):\textsc{Type}
with h-propositional identities. Moreover, there are a canonical homotopy equivalence
\langle\;f_{1}\;|\;g_{1}\;\rangle
:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f_{1}(\gamma,x):A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}:A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g_{1}(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}):A(\gamma)

between
A(\gamma)
and
A^{\prime}(\delta)
relative to
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
and a canonical homotopy equivalence
\langle\;f_{2}\;|\;g_{2}\;\rangle
:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma),y:B(\gamma,x)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f_{2}(\gamma,x,y):B^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma),f_{1}(\gamma,x))

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma),\underline{y}^{\prime}:B^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma),f_{1}(\gamma,x))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g_{2}(\gamma,x,\underline{y}^{\prime}):B(\gamma,x)

between
B(\gamma,x)
and
B^{\prime}(\delta,\underline{x})
relative to the extension
(\textnormal{{f}},\textnormal{{f}}_{m+1};\textnormal{{g}},\textnormal{{g}}_{n+1})
of
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
via
(f_{1};g_{1})
(see
Lemma

3.1.1
), such that the homotopy equivalence
(f^{\Pi};g^{\Pi})
of Lemma
3.2.1
is the given
\langle\;\varphi\;|\;\psi\;\rangle
. Analogously, there are a canonical homotopy equivalence
\langle\;f^{\prime}_{1}\;|\;g^{\prime}_{1}\;\rangle
:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f^{\prime}_{1}(\gamma,x):A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}(\gamma))

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}:A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}(\gamma))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g^{\prime}_{1}(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}):A(\gamma)

between
A(\gamma)
and
A^{\prime}(\delta)
relative to
(\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime};\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime})
and a canonical homotopy equivalence
\langle\;f^{\prime}_{2}\;|\;g^{\prime}_{2}\;\rangle
:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma),y:B(\gamma,x)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f^{\prime}_{2}(\gamma,x,y):B^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}(\gamma),f^{\prime}_{1}(\gamma,x))

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma),\underline{y}^{\prime}:B^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}(\gamma),f^{\prime}_{1}(\gamma,x))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g^{\prime}_{2}(\gamma,x,\underline{y}^{\prime}):B(\gamma,x)

between
B(\gamma,x)
and
B^{\prime}(\delta,\underline{x})
relative to the extension
(\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime},\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}_{m+1};\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime},\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime}_{n+1})
of
(\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime};\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime})
via
(f^{\prime}_{1};g^{\prime}_{1})
(see
Lemma

3.1.1
), such that the homotopy equivalence
(f^{\prime\Pi};g^{\prime\Pi})
of Lemma
3.2.1
is the given
\langle\;\varphi^{\prime}\;|\;\psi^{\prime}\;\rangle
. By inductive hypothesis:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\alpha_{1}(\gamma,x):f_{1}(\gamma,x)=\alpha(\gamma)^{*}f^{\prime}_{1}(\gamma,x)
i.e.
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}:A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}(\gamma))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g_{1}(\gamma,\alpha(\gamma)^{*}\underline{x}^{\prime})=g^{\prime}_{1}(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime})

and:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x:A(\gamma),y:B(\gamma,x)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f_{2}(\gamma,x,y)=(\alpha(\gamma),\alpha_{1}(\gamma,x))^{*}f^{\prime}_{2}(\gamma,x,y)

i.e.
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x:A(\gamma),\underline{y}^{\prime}:B(\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}(\gamma),f^{\prime}_{1}(\gamma,x))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g_{2}(\gamma,x,(\alpha(\gamma),\alpha_{1}(\gamma,x))^{*}\underline{y}^{\prime})=g^{\prime}_{2}(\gamma,x,\underline{y}^{\prime})
.

Then:

\displaystyle g_{2}(\gamma,x,\textnormal{{ev}}(\alpha(\gamma)^{*}\underline{z}^{\prime},f_{1}(\gamma,x)))

\displaystyle=g_{2}(\gamma,x,\alpha_{1}(\gamma,x)^{*}\textnormal{{ev}}(\alpha(\gamma)^{*}\underline{z}^{\prime},\alpha(\gamma)^{*}f^{\prime}_{1}(\gamma,x)))

\displaystyle=g_{2}(\gamma,x,\alpha_{1}(\gamma,x)^{*}(\alpha(\gamma),\textnormal{{r}}(\alpha(\gamma)^{*}f^{\prime}_{1}(\gamma,x)))^{*}\textnormal{{ev}}(\underline{z}^{\prime},f^{\prime}_{1}(\gamma,x)))

\displaystyle=g_{2}(\gamma,x,\alpha_{1}(\gamma,x)^{*}\alpha(\gamma)^{*}\textnormal{{ev}}(\underline{z}^{\prime},f^{\prime}_{1}(\gamma,x)))

\displaystyle=g_{2}(\gamma,x,(\alpha(\gamma),\alpha_{1}(\gamma,x))^{*}\textnormal{{ev}}(\underline{z}^{\prime},f^{\prime}_{1}(\gamma,x)))

\displaystyle=g^{\prime}_{2}(\gamma,x,\textnormal{{ev}}(\underline{z}^{\prime},f^{\prime}_{1}(\gamma,x)))

in context
{\gamma,\underline{z}^{\prime}:[\Pi_{\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta)}B^{\prime}(\delta,\underline{x})](\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}(\gamma))}
, where the first and the fourth equalities follows by (generalised) path induction on
\alpha_{1}(\gamma,x)
and the second and the third by multiple (generalised) path induction on
\alpha(\gamma)
. By propositional
\Pi
-extensionality, we deduce that:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,\underline{z}^{\prime}:[\Pi_{\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\delta)}B^{\prime}(\delta,\underline{x})](\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}(\gamma))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g^{\Pi}(\gamma,\alpha(\gamma)^{*}\underline{z}^{\prime})=g^{\prime\Pi}(\gamma,\underline{z}^{\prime})

and we are done.

If
S(\gamma)
is of the form
\Sigma_{x:A(\gamma)}B(\gamma,x)
for some
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}
with h-propositional identities and some
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;B(\gamma,x):\textsc{Type}
with h-propositional identities then, being
\langle\;\varphi\;|\;\psi\;\rangle
a canonical homotopy equivalence, the type
T(\delta)
needs to be of the form
\Sigma_{x^{\prime}:A^{\prime}(\delta)}B^{\prime}(\delta,x^{\prime})
for some
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A^{\prime}(\delta):\textsc{Type}
with h-propositional identities and some
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta,x^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;B(\delta,x^{\prime}):\textsc{Type}
with h-propositional identities. Moreover, there are a canonical homotopy equivalence
\langle\;f_{1}\;|\;g_{1}\;\rangle
:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f_{1}(\gamma,x):A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}:A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g_{1}(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}):A(\gamma)

between
A(\gamma)
and
A^{\prime}(\delta)
relative to
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
and a canonical homotopy equivalence
\langle\;f_{2}\;|\;g_{2}\;\rangle
:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma),y:B(\gamma,x)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f_{2}(\gamma,x,y):B^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma),f_{1}(\gamma,x))

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma),\underline{y}^{\prime}:B^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma),f_{1}(\gamma,x))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g_{2}(\gamma,x,\underline{y}^{\prime}):B(\gamma,x)

between
B(\gamma,x)
and
B^{\prime}(\delta,\underline{x})
relative to the extension
(\textnormal{{f}},\textnormal{{f}}_{m+1};\textnormal{{g}},\textnormal{{g}}_{n+1})
of
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
via
(f_{1};g_{1})
(Lemma
3.1.1
), such that the homotopy equivalence
(f^{\Sigma};g^{\Sigma})
of Lemma
3.3.1
is the given
\langle\;\varphi\;|\;\psi\;\rangle
. Analogously, there are a canonical homotopy equivalence
\langle\;f^{\prime}_{1}\;|\;g^{\prime}_{1}\;\rangle
:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f^{\prime}_{1}(\gamma,x):A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}(\gamma))

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}:A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}(\gamma))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g^{\prime}_{1}(\gamma,\underline{x}^{\prime}):A(\gamma)

between
A(\gamma)
and
A^{\prime}(\delta)
relative to
(\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime};\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime})
and a canonical homotopy equivalence
\langle\;f^{\prime}_{2}\;|\;g^{\prime}_{2}\;\rangle
:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma),y:B(\gamma,x)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f^{\prime}_{2}(\gamma,x,y):B^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}(\gamma),f^{\prime}_{1}(\gamma,x))

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma,x:A(\gamma),\underline{y}^{\prime}:B^{\prime}(\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}(\gamma),f^{\prime}_{1}(\gamma,x))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g^{\prime}_{2}(\gamma,x,\underline{y}^{\prime}):B(\gamma,x)

between
B(\gamma,x)
and
B^{\prime}(\delta,\underline{x})
relative to the extension
(\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime},\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}_{m+1};\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime},\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime}_{n+1})
of
(\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime};\textnormal{{g}}^{\prime})
via
(f^{\prime}_{1};g^{\prime}_{1})
(Lemma
3.1.1
), such that the homotopy equivalence
(f^{\prime\Sigma};g^{\prime\Sigma})
of Lemma
3.3.1
is the given
\langle\;\varphi^{\prime}\;|\;\psi^{\prime}\;\rangle
. By inductive hypothesis:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\alpha_{1}(\gamma,x):f_{1}(\gamma,x)=\alpha(\gamma)^{*}f^{\prime}_{1}(\gamma,x)

and:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x:A(\gamma),y:B(\gamma,x)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f_{2}(\gamma,x,y)=(\alpha(\gamma),\alpha_{1}(\gamma,x))^{*}f^{\prime}_{2}(\gamma,x,y)

hence:

\displaystyle{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,u:\Sigma_{x:A(\gamma)}B(\gamma,x)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f^{\Sigma}(\gamma,u)

\displaystyle\equiv\langle f_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u),f_{2}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u,\pi_{2}u)\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\alpha(\gamma)^{*}f^{\prime}_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u),\alpha(\gamma)^{*}f^{\prime}_{2}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u,\pi_{2}u)\rangle

\displaystyle=\alpha(\gamma)^{*}\langle f^{\prime}_{1}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u),f^{\prime}_{2}(\gamma,\pi_{1}u,\pi_{2}u)\rangle

\displaystyle\equiv\alpha(\gamma)^{*}f^{\prime\Sigma}(\gamma,u)

where the second propositional equality follows by multiple (generalised) path induction on
\alpha(\gamma)
. We are done.

If
S(\gamma)
is an h-proposition
s_{1}(\gamma)=s_{2}(\gamma)
for some
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}
with h-propositional identities and some
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;s_{1}(\gamma),s_{2}(\gamma):A(\gamma)
then, being
\langle\;\varphi\;|\;\psi\;\rangle
a canonical homotopy equivalence, the type
T(\delta)
needs to be an h-proposition
t_{1}(\delta)=t_{2}(\delta)
for some
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A^{\prime}(\delta):\textsc{Type}
with h-propositional identities and some
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;t_{1}(\delta),t_{2}(\delta):A^{\prime}(\delta)
. We observe that
\varphi(\gamma,p):t_{1}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))=t_{2}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))
and
\varphi^{\prime}(\gamma,p):t_{1}(\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}(\gamma))=t_{2}(\textnormal{{f}}^{\prime}(\gamma))
in context
\gamma,p:s_{1}(\gamma)=s_{2}(\gamma)
, hence
\alpha(\gamma)^{*}\varphi^{\prime}(\gamma,p):t_{1}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))=t_{2}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))
. Since
t_{1}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))=t_{2}(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))
an h-proposition, then
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,p\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\varphi(\gamma,p)=\alpha(\gamma)^{*}\varphi^{\prime}(\gamma,p)
and we are done. ∎

Remark 4.2.7
.

We observe that the soundness of the last paragraph of the previous proof is the key reason why we need to work in the restricted family of type judgements of Definition
4.2.1
.

In
Section

5
a further restriction of the contexts of
PTT
is adopted.

5
Making the syntax of Propositional Type Theory into a category with attributes

In this section we use the syntax of
PTT
in order to define a model of
ETT
. We remind that a type judgement
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;T(\delta):\textsc{Type}
is said to be an
h-set
if every type judgement
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta,x_{1},x_{2}:T(\delta)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;x_{1}=x_{2}:\textsc{Type}
is an h-proposition. We start by defining a further restriction on the type family that we allow to build contexts:

Definition 5.0.1
.

A type judgement
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;T(\delta):\textsc{Type}
in some context
\delta:\Delta
is
h-elementary
if it belongs to the smallest family
\mathcal{F}
of type judgements that satisfies the following clauses:

\succ

a judgement
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;S:\textsc{Type}
(where
S
is an atomic type) belongs to
\mathcal{F}
, whenever
S
is an h-set;

\succ

a judgement
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\Pi_{x:A(\gamma)}B(\gamma,x):\textsc{Type}
, for some
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}
of
\mathcal{F}
and some
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;B(\gamma,x):\textsc{Type}
of
\mathcal{F}
, belongs to
\mathcal{F}
;

\succ

a judgement
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\Sigma_{x:A(\gamma)}B(\gamma,x):\textsc{Type}
, for some
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}
of
\mathcal{F}
and some
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;B(\gamma,x):\textsc{Type}
of
\mathcal{F}
, belongs to
\mathcal{F}
;

\succ

a judgement
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;s_{1}(\gamma)=s_{2}(\gamma):\textsc{Type}
, for some
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}
of
\mathcal{F}
and some
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;s_{1}(\gamma),s_{2}(\gamma):A(\gamma)
, belongs to
\mathcal{F}
.

The h-elementary types and the types with h-propositional identities (see Definition
4.2.1
) differ in their restrictions: while the latter only restrict the identity type formation to when it produces h-propositions, h-elementary types further restrict the atomic types to the h-sets.

Definition 5.0.2
.

Let
\gamma:\Gamma
be a context
\gamma_{1}:\Gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}:\Gamma_{2}(\gamma_{1}),...,\gamma_{n}:\Gamma_{n}(\gamma_{1},...,\gamma_{n-1})
, where
n
might be
0
. For any
i\in\{1,...,n\}
, let
\gamma^{i}
be the context
\gamma_{1},...,\gamma_{i}
. We say that
\gamma
is
h-elementary
if, for every
i\in\{1,...,n\}
, the judgement
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{i}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\Gamma_{i}(\gamma^{i})
is h-elementary.

We recall that in an intensional type theory the family of the h-sets is closed under
\Pi
,
\Sigma
, and
=
operations. The same holds in
PTT
, i.e. whenever
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}
and
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;B(\gamma,x):\textsc{Type}
are h-sets, the judgements:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\Sigma_{x:A(\gamma)}B(x,\gamma)\textnormal{, }{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\Pi_{x:A(\gamma)}B(x,\gamma)\textnormal{, and }{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x,y:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;x=y

are h-sets as well. The proof works formally as in the intensional case. We refer the reader to
[
RS15
, Section 2.3]
and to
[
Uni13
, Chapter 3]
. Therefore, by induction on the complexity of an h-elementary type, we infer that:

Corollary 5.0.3
.

Every h-elementary type is an h-set.

and therefore, again by induction on the complexity of an h-elementary type, one proves that:

Remark 5.0.4
.

Every h-elementary type has h-propositional identities, hence every h-elementary context is a context with h-propositional identities. In particular, the results of
Section

4.2
continue being true for the family of h-elementary types and contexts.

By induction on the complexity of a canonical homotopy equivalence, one proves that:

Lemma 5.0.5
.

Let
\gamma:\Gamma
and
\delta:\Delta
be h-elementary contexts together with a context homotopy equivalence
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
such that
|\gamma:\Gamma|\equiv|\delta:\Delta|
and:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ |\gamma|\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}_{\textnormal{{ext}}}\;\;\;|\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma)|\equiv|\gamma|
( i.e.
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ |\gamma|\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}_{\textnormal{{ext}}}\;\;\;|\textnormal{{g}}(\delta)|\equiv|\gamma|
)

and let
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;S(\gamma):\textsc{Type}
and
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta:\Delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;T(\delta):\textsc{Type}
be h-elementary. Moreover, let
\langle\;\varphi\;|\;\psi\;\rangle
be a canonical homotopy equivalence between
S(\gamma)
and
T(\delta)
relative to
(\textnormal{{f}};\textnormal{{g}})
. Then
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ |\gamma|\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}_{\textnormal{{ext}}}\;\;\;|S(\gamma)|\equiv|T(\delta)|
(
\equiv|T(\textnormal{{f}}(\gamma))|
) and:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ |\gamma|,|s|\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}_{\textnormal{{ext}}}\;\;\;|\varphi(\gamma,s)|\equiv|s|
( i.e.
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ |\gamma|,|t^{\prime}|\equiv|t|\equiv|s|\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}_{\textnormal{{ext}}}\;\;\;|\psi(\delta,t^{\prime})|\equiv|s|
).

and therefore, by induction on the complexity of a canonical context homotopy equivalence, one infers that:

Proposition 5.0.6
.

Let
\gamma:\Gamma
and
\delta:\Delta
be h-elementary contexts together with a canonical context homotopy equivalence
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
from
\gamma
to
\delta
. Then
|\gamma|\equiv|\delta|
and
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ |\gamma|\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}_{\textnormal{{ext}}}\;\;\;|\textnormal{{c}}(\gamma)|=|\gamma|
i.e.
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ |\gamma|\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}_{\textnormal{{ext}}}\;\;\;|\textnormal{{d}}(\delta)|=|\gamma|
.

5.1
The semantic context category

We say that two h-elementary contexts of
PTT
are equivalent if there is a canonical context homotopy equivalence between them. This request defines an equivalence relation: its reflexivity follows by Proposition
4.1.2
, its symmetry follows by Proposition
4.1.4
and its transitivity from Proposition
4.1.8
.

From now on, whenever we speak about contexts and types of
PTT
, we will actually refer to
h-elementary
contexts and types of
PTT
.

Let us assume that we are given two (h-elementary) context morphisms
\gamma\xrightarrow{f(\gamma)}\delta
and
\gamma^{\prime}\xrightarrow{f^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime})}\delta^{\prime}
(of
PTT
). We say that
f(\gamma)
and
f^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime})
are equivalent if and only if:

1.

the contexts
\gamma
and
\gamma^{\prime}
are equivalent;

2.

the contexts
\delta
and
\delta^{\prime}
are equivalent;

3.

if
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
is a canonical context homotopy equivalence
\gamma\to\gamma^{\prime}
and
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}^{\prime}\;\rangle
is one
\delta\to\delta^{\prime}
, then:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\gamma^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\delta^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}(\delta)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{{c}}(\delta)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f(\gamma)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime})}

i.e.
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}(f(\gamma))=f^{\prime}(\textnormal{{c}}(\delta))
, where we observe that this condition does not depend on the choice of
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
and
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}^{\prime}\;\rangle
by Proposition
4.2.5
(we remind that h-elementary contexts have h-propositional identities).

Then we observe that:

Proposition 5.1.1
.

There is a category
\mathcal{C}
such that:

\succ

the objects of
\mathcal{C}
are the equivalence classes
[\gamma:\Gamma]
of h-elementary contexts;

\succ

the arrows
[\gamma:\Gamma]\to[\delta:\Delta]
of
\mathcal{C}
are the equivalence classes
[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\gamma^{\prime}):\Delta^{\prime}]
of morphisms of contexts
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\gamma^{\prime}):\Delta^{\prime}
where
[\gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime}]=[\gamma]
and
[\delta^{\prime}:\Delta^{\prime}]=[\delta]
;

\succ

if we are given arrows:

[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\gamma^{\prime}):\Delta^{\prime}]:[\gamma:\Gamma]\to[\delta:\Delta]
and
[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime\prime}:\Delta^{\prime\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g(\delta^{\prime\prime}):\Omega^{\prime}]:[\delta:\Delta]\to[\omega:\Omega]

then, whenever
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
is a canonical homotopy equivalence
\delta^{\prime}\to\delta^{\prime\prime}
, the composition arrow:

[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime\prime}:\Delta^{\prime\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g(\delta^{\prime\prime}):\Omega^{\prime}]\circ[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\gamma^{\prime}):\Delta^{\prime}]:[\gamma:\Gamma]\to[\omega:\Omega]

is the arrow
[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g(\textnormal{{c}}(f(\gamma^{\prime}))):\Omega^{\prime}].

Moreover, the category
\mathcal{C}
has a terminal object.

Proof.

Observe that the composition operation is in fact well-defined: if we are given:

[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime\prime}):\Delta^{\prime\prime\prime}]=[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\gamma^{\prime}):\Delta^{\prime}]
and
[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{iv}:\Delta^{iv}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g^{\prime}(\delta^{iv}):\Omega^{\prime\prime}]=[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime\prime}:\Delta^{\prime\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g(\delta^{\prime\prime}):\Omega^{\prime}]

then:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\gamma^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\delta^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\delta^{\prime\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\omega^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\gamma^{\prime\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\delta^{iv}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\omega^{\prime\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime\prime}(\delta^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{{c}}^{iv}(\gamma^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{{c}}(\delta^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f(\gamma^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g(\delta^{\prime\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime}(\delta^{iv})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime\prime\prime}(\delta^{\prime\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{{c}}^{v}(\omega^{\prime})}

for any choice of canonical context homotopy equivalences:

\displaystyle\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}^{\prime}\;\rangle

\displaystyle:\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}\to\delta^{iv}

\displaystyle\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime\prime}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}^{\prime\prime}\;\rangle

\displaystyle:\delta^{\prime}\to\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}

\displaystyle\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime\prime\prime}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}^{\prime\prime\prime}\;\rangle

\displaystyle:\delta^{\prime\prime}\to\delta^{iv}

\displaystyle\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}^{iv}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}^{iv}\;\rangle

\displaystyle:\gamma^{\prime}\to\gamma^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}^{v}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}^{v}\;\rangle

\displaystyle:\omega^{\prime}\to\omega^{\prime\prime}

where the propositional commutativity of the inner square is a consequence of Proposition
4.1.8
and Proposition
4.2.5
. Hence:

[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g^{\prime}(\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}(f^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime\prime}))):\Omega^{\prime\prime}]=[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g(\textnormal{{c}}(f(\gamma^{\prime}))):\Omega^{\prime}]

and we are done. This operation is associative and the identity of
[\gamma:\Gamma]
is the class
[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma]
, hence
\mathcal{C}
is actually a category.

We observe that a terminal object of
\mathcal{C}
is the class
[\;\_:\_\;]
represented by the empty context. In fact, whenever we are given an object
[\gamma:\Gamma]
of
\mathcal{C}
, an arrow
[\gamma:\Gamma]\to[\;\_:\_\;]
is the one represented by the judgement
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\_:\_
. Moreover, it is unique: if we are given an arrow
[\gamma:\Gamma]\to[\;\_:\_\;]
represented by some judgement
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\_:\_
, then the diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\_}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\gamma^{\prime}}}
c
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\_}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\_}

commutes (for every
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
canonical
\gamma\to\gamma^{\prime}
) even strictly by the terminality of
\_
in the category of contexts of
PTT
, hence
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\_
represents
[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\_]
. ∎

We conclude the current subsection with the following:

Remark 5.1.2
.

1.

By Proposition
4.1.2
, if we are given two parallel morphisms of contexts
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\gamma),f^{\prime}(\gamma):\Delta
, then
[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\gamma)]=[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f^{\prime}(\gamma)]
precisely when
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\gamma)=f^{\prime}(\gamma)
.

2.

By Proposition
4.1.4
and by Proposition
4.1.8
, a judgement
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\gamma^{\prime}):\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
represents
[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma]
(where
[\gamma^{\prime}]=[\gamma]=[\gamma^{\prime\prime}]
) if and only if
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\gamma^{\prime})=\textnormal{{c}}(\gamma^{\prime})
for some
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
between
\gamma^{\prime}
and
\gamma^{\prime\prime}
. In particular, if
\gamma^{\prime}\equiv\gamma^{\prime\prime}
then
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\gamma^{\prime})
represents
[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma]
if and only if
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\gamma^{\prime})=\gamma^{\prime}
, by Proposition
4.1.2
. Therefore
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma^{\prime}
represents
[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma]
whenever
[\gamma^{\prime}]=[\gamma]
.

3.

If we are given an arrow
[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\gamma^{\prime}):\Delta^{\prime}]:[\gamma:\Gamma]\to[\delta:\Delta]
then, if
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
and
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}^{\prime}\;\rangle
are canonical between
\gamma
and
\gamma^{\prime}
and between
\delta^{\prime}
and
\delta
respectively, we infer by 1. (or by Proposition
4.1.4
and by Proposition
4.2.5
) that:

[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\gamma^{\prime}):\Delta^{\prime}]=[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}(f(\textnormal{{c}}(\gamma))):\Delta].

Hence without loss of generality we can always assume that we are given a representative of
[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\gamma^{\prime}):\Delta^{\prime}]
of the form
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f^{\prime}(\gamma):\Delta
.

4.

If we are given an arrow
[\gamma:\Gamma]\to[\delta:\Delta]
represented by
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\gamma):\Delta
and an arrow
[\delta:\Delta]\to[\omega:\Omega]
represented by
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta:\Delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g(\delta):\Omega
, then their composition is represented by
g(\textnormal{{c}}(f(\gamma)))
for any canonical homotopy equivalence
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
between
\delta
and itself. Hence
g(f(\gamma))
represents their composition as well, by Proposition
4.1.2
, Proposition
4.2.5
and 1. of Remark
5.1.2
.

5.2
The presheaf of semantic types

In this subsection we define a presheaf of semantic types
tp
associated to the category
\mathcal{C}
of Proposition
5.1.1
. If
\gamma:\Gamma
and
\gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime}
are equivalent h-elementary contexts and
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}
and
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime}):\textsc{Type}
are h-elementary then we say that the judgements
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}
and
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime}):\textsc{Type}
are equivalent if there is a canonical homotopy equivalence between
A(\gamma)
and
A^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime})
relative to some canonical context homotopy equivalence between
\gamma
and
\gamma^{\prime}
. Equivalently (see Lemma
3.1.1
and the notion of canonical context homotopy equivalence), if the contexts
\gamma,x:A(\gamma)
and
\gamma^{\prime},x^{\prime}:A(\gamma^{\prime})
are equivalent.

\succ

If we are given an h-elementary context
\gamma:\Gamma
, we define
\textsc{tp}[\gamma:\Gamma]
as the family of the classes
[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma^{\prime}):\textsc{Type}]
where
[\gamma^{\prime}]=[\gamma]
and
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma^{\prime}):\textsc{Type}
is h-elementary.

\succ

If we are given an arrow
[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\gamma^{\prime}):\Delta^{\prime}]:[\gamma:\Gamma]\to[\delta:\Delta]
, we define the map
\textsc{tp}[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\gamma^{\prime}):\Delta^{\prime}]:\textsc{tp}[\delta:\Delta]\to\textsc{tp}[\gamma:\Gamma]
as the one such that:

[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\delta^{\prime\prime}):\textsc{Type}]\mapsto[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}(f(\textnormal{{c}}(\gamma)))):\textsc{Type}]

where
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
and
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}^{\prime}\;\rangle
are canonical between
\gamma
and
\gamma^{\prime}
and between
\delta^{\prime}
and
\delta^{\prime\prime}
respectively. This relation is in fact a mapping:

–

Assuming that
[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime\prime}):\Delta^{\prime\prime\prime}]=[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\gamma^{\prime}):\Delta^{\prime}]
and letting
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime\prime}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}^{\prime\prime}\;\rangle
and
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime\prime\prime}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}^{\prime\prime\prime}\;\rangle
be canonical between
\gamma
and
\gamma^{\prime\prime}
and between
\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}
and
\delta^{\prime\prime}
respectively, we are left to verify that:

[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime\prime\prime}(f^{\prime}(\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime\prime}(\gamma)))):\textsc{Type}]=[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}(f(\textnormal{{c}}(\gamma)))):\textsc{Type}].

Let
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}^{iv}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}^{iv}\;\rangle
and
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}^{v}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}^{v}\;\rangle
be canonical between
\gamma^{\prime}
and
\gamma^{\prime\prime}
and between
\delta^{\prime}
and
\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}
. Then:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\gamma^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\delta^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\delta^{\prime\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\gamma^{\prime\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f(\gamma^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime\prime})}
c
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime\prime\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{{c}}^{iv}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{{c}}^{v}}

by Proposition
4.1.8
. By Proposition
4.1.2
, there is a canonical context homotopy equivalence
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}^{vi}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}^{vi}\;\rangle
between
\gamma
and itself such that
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}^{vi}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}^{vi}\;\rangle
is pairwise homotopic to
({\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma;{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma)
. Hence:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\delta^{\prime\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{{c}}^{vi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}(f(\textnormal{{c}}(\gamma)))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime\prime\prime}(f^{\prime}(\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime\prime}(\gamma)))}

and therefore we are done by Lemma
4.1.9
.

–

Assuming that
[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime\prime\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}):\textsc{Type}]=[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\delta^{\prime\prime}):\textsc{Type}]
, and letting
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime\prime}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}^{\prime\prime}\;\rangle
be canonical between
\delta^{\prime}
and
\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}
, we are left to verify that:

[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime\prime}(f(\textnormal{{c}}(\gamma)))):\textsc{Type}]=[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}(f(\textnormal{{c}}(\gamma)))):\textsc{Type}].

Let
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime\prime\prime}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}^{\prime\prime\prime}\;\rangle
be a canonical homotopy equivalence between
\delta^{\prime\prime}
and
\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}
. Then:

\displaystyle[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}(\delta)):\textsc{Type}]

\displaystyle=[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\delta^{\prime\prime}):\textsc{Type}]

\displaystyle=[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime\prime\prime}(\delta^{\prime\prime})):\textsc{Type}]

\displaystyle=[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A^{\prime}(\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime\prime}(\delta^{\prime}))]

by Corollary
4.1.10
, by Lemma
4.1.11
and by Lemma
4.1.9
respectively. Then we are done by Lemma
4.1.12
with
\delta\equiv\delta^{\prime}
and
a(\gamma)\equiv f(\textnormal{{c}}(\gamma))
.

In the remainder of the current subsection we verify that:

Proposition 5.2.1
.

The mapping
tp
is a functor.

Proof.

\succ

Let us consider the diagram:

[\gamma]\xrightarrow{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\gamma^{\prime}):\Delta^{\prime}]}[\delta]\xrightarrow{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;g(\delta^{\prime\prime}):\Omega^{\prime}]}[\omega]

and let
[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \omega^{\prime\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\omega^{\prime\prime}):\textsc{Type}]
be in
\textsc{tp}[\omega]
. Let us consider canonical context homotopy equivalences:

\displaystyle\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle

\displaystyle:\gamma\to\gamma^{\prime}

\displaystyle\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}^{\prime}\;\rangle

\displaystyle:\delta^{\prime}\to\delta

\displaystyle\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime\prime}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}^{\prime\prime}\;\rangle

\displaystyle:\delta\to\delta^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime\prime\prime}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}^{\prime\prime\prime}\;\rangle

\displaystyle:\omega^{\prime}\to\omega^{\prime\prime\prime}

\displaystyle\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}^{iv}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}^{iv}\;\rangle

\displaystyle:\delta^{\prime}\to\delta^{\prime\prime}.

Then, to verify that
tp
preserves the composition, it is enough to verify that:

[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime\prime\prime}(g(\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime\prime}(\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}(f(\textnormal{{c}}(\gamma)))))))]=[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime\prime\prime}(g(\textnormal{{c}}^{iv}(f(\textnormal{{c}}(\gamma))))))]

which is in fact true by Proposition
4.1.8
and Lemma
4.1.9
.

\succ

Let
[\gamma]
be a context and let
[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma^{\prime}):\textsc{Type}]
be in
\textsc{tp}[\gamma]
. As
[\gamma^{\prime}]=[\gamma]
, the morphism
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma^{\prime}
represents the identity of
[\gamma]
(see 2. of Remark
5.1.2
). Let us consider canonical context homotopy equivalences:

\displaystyle\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle

\displaystyle:\gamma^{\prime}\to\gamma^{\prime}

\displaystyle\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}^{\prime}\;\rangle

\displaystyle:\gamma^{\prime}\to\gamma^{\prime}.

Then
[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}(\textnormal{{c}}(\gamma^{\prime})))]=[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma^{\prime})]
by Corollary
4.1.10
, hence
tp
preserves the identities and we are done.∎

We end the current subsection with the following:

Remark 5.2.2
.

1.

By
4.1.10
, if we are given a semantic type
[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma^{\prime}):\textsc{Type}]
in semantic context
[\gamma:\Gamma]
, then:

[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\textnormal{{c}}(\gamma)):\textsc{Type}]=[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma^{\prime}):\textsc{Type}]

for every
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
canonical
\gamma\to\gamma^{\prime}
. Hence we can always assume that we are given a representative of a semantic type in semantic context
[\gamma:\Gamma]
whose context coincides with
\gamma
itself.

2.

If we are given an arrow
[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\gamma^{\prime}):\Delta^{\prime}]:[\gamma:\Gamma]\to[\delta:\Delta]
, we observe that the image of
[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\delta^{\prime\prime}):\textsc{Type}]
via
\textsc{tp}[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\gamma^{\prime}):\Delta^{\prime}]:\textsc{tp}[\delta:\Delta]\to\textsc{tp}[\gamma:\Gamma]
, which is
[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}(f(\textnormal{{c}}(\gamma)))):\textsc{Type}]
by definition, also coincides with:

[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}(f(\gamma^{\prime}))):\textsc{Type}]

by
4.1.10
, being
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
and
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}^{\prime}\;\rangle
canonical between
\gamma
and
\gamma^{\prime}
and between
\delta^{\prime}
and
\delta^{\prime\prime}
. We use this particular presentation in
Section

5.3
.

5.3
The semantic context extension

The semantic context extension
-.-
associated to
\mathcal{C}
and
tp
(see
Sections

5.1
and
5.2
respectively) is defined as follows:

\succ

An object
([\gamma:\Gamma],[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma^{\prime}):\textsc{Type}])
of
\int\textsc{tp}
is sent to:

[\gamma:\Gamma].[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma^{\prime}):\textsc{Type}]:=[\gamma^{\prime},x:A(\gamma^{\prime})].

This mapping is well-defined because
[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma^{\prime}):\textsc{Type}]=[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime\prime}):\textsc{Type}]
precisely when
[\gamma^{\prime},x:A(\gamma^{\prime})]=[\gamma^{\prime\prime},\underline{x}:A^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime\prime})]
, by the notion of canonical context homotopy equivalence.

\succ

An arrow
([\delta:\Delta],[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;B(\delta^{\prime}):\textsc{Type}])\to([\gamma:\Gamma],[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma^{\prime}):\textsc{Type}])
in
\int\textsc{tp}
is an arrow
[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime\prime}:\Delta^{\prime\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\delta^{\prime\prime}):\Gamma^{\prime\prime}]
such that:

\textsc{tp}[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime\prime}:\Delta^{\prime\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\delta^{\prime\prime}):\Gamma^{\prime\prime}]:[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma^{\prime}):\textsc{Type}]\mapsto[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;B(\delta^{\prime}):\textsc{Type}].

Hence, if
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
is a canonical context homotopy equivalence
\gamma^{\prime\prime}\to\gamma^{\prime}
, then:

[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;B(\delta^{\prime}):\textsc{Type}]=[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\textnormal{{c}}(f(\delta^{\prime\prime}))):\textsc{Type}]

by 2. or Remark
5.2.2
. Moreover:

[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma^{\prime}):\textsc{Type}]=[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\textnormal{{c}}(\gamma^{\prime\prime})):\textsc{Type}]

by Corollary
4.1.10
. We stipulate that
-.-
maps such an arrow of
\int\textsc{tp}
to the arrow:

[\delta^{\prime\prime},\underline{x}:A(\textnormal{{c}}(f(\delta^{\prime\prime})))]\xrightarrow{[\;{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime\prime},\;\underline{x}\;\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\delta^{\prime\prime}),\;\underline{x}\;]}[\gamma^{\prime\prime},x:A(\textnormal{{c}}(\gamma^{\prime\prime}))].

Let us prove that this relation is actually a map. Let us consider a representative:
