[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime\prime\prime}:\Delta^{\prime\prime\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}):\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}]=[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime\prime}:\Delta^{\prime\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\delta^{\prime\prime}):\Gamma^{\prime\prime}]

and let
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}^{\prime}\;\rangle
be canonical
\gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}\to\gamma^{\prime}
. We are left to verify that the corresponding:

[\delta^{\prime\prime\prime},\underline{x}^{\prime}:A(\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}(f^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime})))]\xrightarrow{[\;{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime\prime\prime},\;\underline{x}^{\prime}\;\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}),\;\underline{x}^{\prime}\;]}[\gamma^{\prime\prime\prime},x^{\prime}:A(\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}))]

satisfies:

[\;{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime\prime},\;\underline{x}\;\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\delta^{\prime\prime}),\;\underline{x}\;]=[\;{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime\prime\prime},\;\underline{x}^{\prime}\;\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}),\;\underline{x}^{\prime}\;].

Let
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime\prime}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}^{\prime\prime}\;\rangle
and
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime\prime\prime}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}^{\prime\prime\prime}\;\rangle
be canonical
\delta^{\prime\prime}\to\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}
and
\gamma^{\prime\prime}\to\gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}
and let us consider the diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\delta^{\prime\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\gamma^{\prime\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\gamma^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f(\delta^{\prime\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime})}
c
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime\prime\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta(\delta^{\prime\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha(\gamma^{\prime\prime})}

where
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\alpha(\gamma^{\prime\prime}):\textnormal{{c}}(\gamma^{\prime\prime})=\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}(\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime\prime\prime}(\gamma^{\prime\prime}))
exists by Proposition
4.1.8
and Proposition
4.2.5
and
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\beta(\delta^{\prime\prime}):\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime\prime\prime}(f(\delta^{\prime\prime}))=f^{\prime}(\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime\prime}(\delta^{\prime\prime}))
exists since
f(\delta^{\prime\prime})
and
f^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime})
represent the same arrow of
\mathcal{C}
. If:

\omega(\delta^{\prime\prime})\equiv\alpha(f(\delta^{\prime\prime}))\bullet\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}(\beta(\delta^{\prime\prime})):\textnormal{{c}}(f(\delta^{\prime\prime}))=\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}(f^{\prime}(\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime\prime}(\delta^{\prime\prime})))

then by Lemma
4.1.9
there exists a canonical homotopy equivalence:

\langle\;\varphi\;|\;\psi\;\rangle

between
A(\textnormal{{c}}(f(\delta^{\prime\prime})))
and
A(\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}(f^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime})))
relative to
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime\prime}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}^{\prime\prime}\;\rangle
and such that:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime\prime},\underline{x}:A(\textnormal{{c}}(f(\delta^{\prime\prime})))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\omega(\delta^{\prime\prime})^{*}\varphi(\delta^{\prime\prime},\underline{x})=\underline{x}.

Anologously, again by Lemma
4.1.9
there is a canonical homotopy equivalence
\langle\;\varphi^{\prime}\;|\;\psi^{\prime}\;\rangle
between
A(\textnormal{{c}}(\gamma^{\prime\prime}))
and
A(\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}))
relative to
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime\prime}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}^{\prime\prime}\;\rangle
and such that:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime\prime},x:A(\textnormal{{c}}(\gamma^{\prime\prime}))\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\alpha(\gamma^{\prime\prime})^{*}\varphi^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime\prime},x)=x.

By the notion of canonical context homotopy equivalence and by Lemma
3.1.1
, we obtain canonical context homotopy equivalences:

\displaystyle{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime\prime},\underline{x}:A(\textnormal{{c}}(f(\delta^{\prime\prime})))

\displaystyle{\penalty\displaystyle\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime\prime}(\delta^{\prime\prime}):\Delta^{\prime\prime\prime},\;\varphi(\delta^{\prime\prime},\underline{x}):A(\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}(f^{\prime}(\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime\prime}(\delta^{\prime\prime}))))

\displaystyle{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime\prime},x:A(\textnormal{{c}}(\gamma^{\prime\prime}))

\displaystyle{\penalty\displaystyle\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime\prime\prime}(\gamma^{\prime\prime}):\Gamma^{\prime\prime\prime},\;\varphi^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime\prime},x):A(\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}(\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime\prime\prime}(\gamma^{\prime\prime})))

between
\delta^{\prime\prime},\underline{x}:A(\textnormal{{c}}(f(\delta^{\prime\prime})))
and
\delta^{\prime\prime\prime},\underline{x}^{\prime}:A(\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}(f^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime})))
and between
\gamma^{\prime\prime},x:A(\textnormal{{c}}(\gamma^{\prime\prime}))
and
\gamma^{\prime\prime\prime},x^{\prime}:A(\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime\prime\prime}))
respectively. Therefore, we are left to verify that:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\delta^{\prime\prime},\underline{x}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\gamma^{\prime\prime},x}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\delta^{\prime\prime\prime},\underline{x}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\gamma^{\prime\prime\prime},x^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime\prime}(\delta^{\prime\prime}),\;\varphi(\delta^{\prime\prime},\underline{x})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime\prime\prime}(\gamma^{\prime\prime}),\;\varphi^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime\prime},x)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f(\delta^{\prime\prime}),\;\underline{x}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}),\;\underline{x}^{\prime}}

i.e. that:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime\prime},\underline{x}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime\prime\prime}(f(\delta^{\prime\prime})),\;\varphi^{\prime}(f(\delta^{\prime\prime}),\underline{x})=f^{\prime}(\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime\prime}(\delta^{\prime\prime})),\;\varphi(\delta^{\prime\prime},\underline{x}).

Since
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\beta(\delta^{\prime\prime}):\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime\prime\prime}(f(\delta^{\prime\prime}))=f^{\prime}(\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime\prime}(\delta^{\prime\prime}))
, we are left to verify that
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime\prime},\underline{x}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\varphi^{\prime}(f(\delta^{\prime\prime}),\underline{x})=\beta(\delta^{\prime\prime})^{*}\varphi(\delta^{\prime\prime},\underline{x})
. But since
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime\prime},\underline{x}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\beta(\delta^{\prime\prime})^{*}\varphi(\delta^{\prime\prime},\underline{x})=\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}(\beta(\delta^{\prime\prime}))^{*}\varphi(\delta^{\prime\prime},\underline{x})
by multiple (generalised) path induction on
\beta(\delta^{\prime\prime})
, we are left to verify that:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime\prime},\underline{x}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\varphi^{\prime}(f(\delta^{\prime\prime}),\underline{x})=\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}(\beta(\delta^{\prime\prime}))^{*}\varphi(\delta^{\prime\prime},\underline{x}).

This is equivalent to verifying that:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime\prime},\underline{x}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\alpha(f(\delta^{\prime\prime}))^{*}\varphi^{\prime}(f(\delta^{\prime\prime}),\underline{x})=\alpha(f(\delta^{\prime\prime}))^{*}\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}(\beta(\delta^{\prime\prime}))^{*}\varphi(\delta^{\prime\prime},\underline{x})

and this is true as
\alpha(f(\delta^{\prime\prime}))^{*}\varphi^{\prime}(f(\delta^{\prime\prime}),\underline{x})=\underline{x}
and

\alpha(f(\delta^{\prime\prime}))^{*}\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}(\beta(\delta^{\prime\prime}))^{*}\varphi(\delta^{\prime\prime},\underline{x})=\omega(\delta^{\prime\prime})^{*}\varphi(\delta^{\prime\prime},\underline{x})=\underline{x}.

We are left to verify that:

Proposition 5.3.1
.

The mapping
-.-
defines a functor.

Proof.

\succ

Suppose that we are given two arrows:

\displaystyle([\omega:\Omega],[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \omega^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;C(\omega^{\prime}):\textsc{Type}])\xrightarrow{[{g(\omega^{\prime\prime}):\Delta^{\prime\prime\prime}}]}([\delta:\Delta],[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;B(\delta^{\prime}):\textsc{Type}])

\displaystyle([\delta:\Delta],[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;B(\delta^{\prime}):\textsc{Type}])\xrightarrow{[{f(\delta^{\prime\prime}):\Gamma^{\prime\prime}}]}([\gamma:\Gamma],[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma^{\prime}):\textsc{Type}])

in
\int\textsc{tp}
. We can rewrite the objects as follows:

\displaystyle([\gamma:\Gamma],[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma^{\prime}):\textsc{Type}])

\displaystyle=([\gamma:\Gamma],[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\textnormal{{c}}(\gamma^{\prime\prime})):\textsc{Type}])

\displaystyle([\delta:\Delta],[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;B(\delta^{\prime}):\textsc{Type}])

\displaystyle=([\delta:\Delta],[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\textnormal{{c}}(f(\delta^{\prime\prime}))):\textsc{Type}])

\displaystyle([\omega:\Omega],[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \omega^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;C(\omega^{\prime}):\textsc{Type}])

\displaystyle=([\omega:\Omega],[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \omega^{\prime\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\textnormal{{c}}(f(\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}(g(\omega^{\prime\prime}))))):\textsc{Type}])

for some
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
and
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}^{\prime}\;\rangle
canonical
\gamma^{\prime\prime}\to\gamma^{\prime}
and
\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}\to\delta^{\prime\prime}
respectively. The composition of
\int\textsc{tp}
sends this diagram to the arrow
[f(\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}(g(\omega^{\prime\prime}))):\Gamma^{\prime\prime}]
of the form:

([\omega:\Omega],[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \omega^{\prime\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\textnormal{{c}}(f(\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}(g(\omega^{\prime\prime}))))):\textsc{Type}])\to([\gamma:\Gamma],[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\textnormal{{c}}(\gamma^{\prime\prime})):\textsc{Type}]).

Now, the arrows
[f(\delta^{\prime\prime})]
,
[g(\omega^{\prime\prime})]
and
[f(\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}(g(\omega^{\prime\prime})))]
are sent by
-.-
to the arrows:

\displaystyle[\delta^{\prime\prime},\underline{x}:A(\textnormal{{c}}(f(\delta^{\prime\prime})))]

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{[f(\delta^{\prime\prime}),\underline{x}]}[\gamma^{\prime\prime},x:A(\textnormal{{c}}(\gamma^{\prime\prime}))]

\displaystyle[\omega^{\prime\prime},\underline{\underline{x}}:A(\textnormal{{c}}(f(\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}(g(\omega^{\prime\prime})))))]

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{[g(\omega^{\prime\prime}),\underline{\underline{x}}]}[\delta^{\prime\prime\prime},\underline{x}^{\prime}:A(\textnormal{{c}}(f(\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}))))]

\displaystyle[\omega^{\prime\prime},\underline{\underline{x}}:A(\textnormal{{c}}(f(\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}(g(\omega^{\prime\prime})))))]

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{[f(\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}(g(\omega^{\prime\prime}))),\underline{\underline{x}}]}[\gamma^{\prime\prime},x:A(\textnormal{{c}}(\gamma^{\prime\prime}))]

of
\mathcal{C}
respectively. In order to conclude that
-.-
actually preserves the composition, we are left to verify that the composition of the first and the second of these yields the third. By Corollary
4.1.10
, there is
\langle\;\varphi\;|\;\psi\;\rangle
canonical between
A(\textnormal{{c}}(f(\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}))))
and
A(\textnormal{{c}}(f(\delta^{\prime\prime})))
relative to
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}^{\prime}\;\rangle
and such that
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime\prime\prime},\underline{x}^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\varphi(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime},\underline{x}^{\prime})=\underline{x}^{\prime}
. Hence, by Lemma
3.1.1
and by the notion of canonical context homotopy equivalence, there is a canonical context homotopy equivalence
\delta^{\prime\prime\prime},\underline{x}^{\prime}\to\delta^{\prime\prime},\underline{x}
whose first component is
\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}),\varphi(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime},\underline{x}^{\prime})
and therefore the composition of
[f(\delta^{\prime\prime}),\underline{x}]\circ[g(\omega^{\prime\prime}),\underline{\underline{x}}]
is represented by:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \omega^{\prime\prime},\underline{\underline{x}}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}(g(\omega^{\prime\prime}))),\varphi(g(\omega^{\prime\prime}),\underline{\underline{x}})

which is in fact propositionally equal to
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \omega^{\prime\prime},\underline{\underline{x}}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\textnormal{{c}}^{\prime}(g(\omega^{\prime\prime}))),\underline{\underline{x}}
since
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \omega^{\prime\prime},\underline{\underline{x}}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\varphi(g(\omega^{\prime\prime}),\underline{\underline{x}})=\underline{\underline{x}}
. We are done by 1. of Remark
5.1.2
.

\succ

By 2. of Remark
5.1.2
, the identity over
([\gamma],[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma^{\prime}):\textsc{Type}])
is represented by the judgement
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma^{\prime}
. By definition, it is sent by
-.-
to the arrow:

[\gamma^{\prime},x^{\prime}:A(\textnormal{{c}}(\gamma^{\prime}))]\xrightarrow{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime},x^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma^{\prime},x^{\prime}]}[\gamma^{\prime},x^{\prime}:A(\textnormal{{c}}(\gamma^{\prime}))]

for some
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
canonical
\gamma^{\prime}\to\gamma^{\prime}
. This is in fact the identity of
[\gamma^{\prime},x^{\prime}:A(\textnormal{{c}}(\gamma^{\prime}))]=[\gamma^{\prime},x:A(\gamma^{\prime})]
. Hence identities are preserved and we are done.∎

5.4
The display map family

We define a cartesian natural transformation
P
from the semantic context extension and the projection, as functors
\int\textsc{tp}\to\mathcal{C}
. Whenever
([\gamma],[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma^{\prime}):\textsc{Type}])
is an object of
\int\textsc{tp}
then let the component of
P
in
([\gamma],[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma^{\prime}):\textsc{Type}])
be the arrow:

[\gamma^{\prime},x:A(\gamma^{\prime})]\xrightarrow{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime},x\;\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\;\gamma^{\prime}]}[\gamma^{\prime}].

The mapping
P
happens to be well-defined: as long as
[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime\prime}):\textsc{Type}]=[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma^{\prime}):\textsc{Type}]
then the diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\gamma^{\prime},x}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\gamma^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\gamma^{\prime\prime},\underline{x}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\gamma^{\prime\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{{c}}(\gamma^{\prime}),\varphi(\gamma^{\prime},x)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{{c}}(\gamma^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma^{\prime\prime}}

commutes even strictly whenever
\langle\;\varphi\;|\;\psi\;\rangle
is canonical
A(\gamma^{\prime})\to A^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime\prime})
relative to some canonical
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
between
\gamma^{\prime}
and
\gamma^{\prime\prime}
. Let us verify that:

Proposition 5.4.1
.

The family
P
is natural and cartesian.

Proof.

\succ

Naturality.
Let us consider an arrow:

[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime\prime}:\Delta^{\prime\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\delta^{\prime\prime}):\Gamma^{\prime\prime}]:([\delta:\Delta],[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;B(\delta^{\prime}):\textsc{Type}])\to([\gamma:\Gamma],[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma^{\prime}):\textsc{Type}])

in
\int\textsc{tp}
. By 3. of Remark
5.1.2
, we can assume without loss of generality that
\delta^{\prime\prime}\equiv\delta^{\prime}
and
\gamma^{\prime\prime}\equiv\gamma^{\prime}
. If
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
is canonical between
\gamma^{\prime}
and itself, then:

\displaystyle[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma^{\prime}):\textsc{Type}]

\displaystyle=[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\textnormal{{c}}(\gamma^{\prime})):\textsc{Type}]

\displaystyle[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;B(\delta^{\prime}):\textsc{Type}]

\displaystyle=[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\textnormal{{c}}(f(\delta^{\prime}))):\textsc{Type}]

Then the images of
[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\delta)^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime}]
via the semantic context extension and via the projection admit the presentations:

\displaystyle[\delta^{\prime},x^{\prime}:A(\textnormal{{c}}(f(\delta^{\prime})))]

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime},x^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\delta^{\prime}),x^{\prime}]}[\gamma^{\prime},x:A(\textnormal{{c}}(\gamma^{\prime}))]

\displaystyle[\delta^{\prime}]

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\delta^{\prime})]}[\gamma^{\prime}]

and we are left to verify that they commute with
P
, in order to conclude the naturality of
P
itself. By considering the representatives
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime},x^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\delta^{\prime}
and
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime},x\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma^{\prime}
of the
[\delta^{\prime},x^{\prime}]
-component and the
[\gamma^{\prime},x]
-component of
P
respectively, we are done by 1. and 4. of Remark
5.1.2
.

\succ

Cartesianity.
Again, let us consider an arrow:

[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime}:\Delta^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\delta^{\prime}):\Gamma^{\prime}]:([\delta:\Delta],[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;B(\delta^{\prime}):\textsc{Type}])\to([\gamma:\Gamma],[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma^{\prime}):\textsc{Type}])

in
\int\textsc{tp}
(the choice of its representative is justified by 3. of Remark
5.1.2
) and let us verify that the commutative square:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\delta^{\prime},x^{\prime}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\gamma^{\prime},x]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\delta^{\prime}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\gamma^{\prime}]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[f(\delta^{\prime}),x^{\prime}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[f(\delta^{\prime})]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[\delta^{\prime}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[\gamma^{\prime}]}

of
\mathcal{C}
is a pullback of
\mathcal{C}
, being
x^{\prime}:A(\textnormal{{c}}(f(\delta^{\prime})))
and
x:A(\textnormal{{c}}(\gamma^{\prime}))
and being
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
canonical
\gamma\to\gamma^{\prime}
. Hence, let us assume that we are given two arrows
\chi:[\omega]\to[\delta^{\prime}]
and
\chi^{\prime}:[\omega]\to[\gamma^{\prime},x]
of
\mathcal{C}
such that:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\omega]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\gamma^{\prime},x]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\delta^{\prime}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\gamma^{\prime}]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[f(\delta^{\prime})]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[\gamma^{\prime}]}

commutes. By 3. of Remark
5.1.2
there are representatives of the form:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \omega\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\alpha_{1}(\omega):\Delta^{\prime}
and
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \omega\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\alpha_{2}(\omega):\Gamma^{\prime},\alpha_{3}(\omega):A(\textnormal{{c}}(\alpha_{2}(\omega)))

of
\chi
and
\chi^{\prime}
respectively and
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \omega\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;p(\omega):f(\alpha_{1}(\omega))=\alpha_{2}(\omega)
, by 1. and 4. of Remark
5.1.2
. Therefore
p(\omega)^{*}\alpha_{3}(\omega):A(\textnormal{{c}}(f(\alpha_{1}(\omega))))
and we obtain a morphism of contexts:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \omega\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\alpha_{1}(\omega):\Delta^{\prime},\;p(\omega)^{*}\alpha_{3}(\omega):A(\textnormal{{c}}(f(\alpha_{1}(\omega))))

which represents a morphism of semantic contexts
\underline{\chi}:[\omega]\to[\delta^{\prime},x^{\prime}]
in
\mathcal{C}
. Post-composing
\underline{\chi}
via
[\delta^{\prime},x^{\prime}]\xrightarrow{[\delta^{\prime}]}[\delta^{\prime}]
and via
[\delta^{\prime},x^{\prime}]\xrightarrow{[f(\delta^{\prime}),x^{\prime}]}[\gamma^{\prime},x]
we get morphisms
[\omega]\to[\delta^{\prime}]
and
[\omega]\to[\gamma^{\prime},x]
respectively represented by:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \omega\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\alpha_{1}(\omega):\Delta^{\prime}
and
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \omega\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\alpha_{1}(\omega)):\Gamma^{\prime},\;p(\omega)^{*}\alpha_{3}(\omega):A(\textnormal{{c}}(f(\alpha_{1}(\omega))))

respectively (by 4. of Remark
5.1.2
). The former is clearly
\chi
, while the latter is
\chi^{\prime}
because:

\displaystyle{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \omega

\displaystyle{\penalty\displaystyle\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;p(\omega):f(\alpha_{1}(\omega))=\alpha_{2}(\omega)

\displaystyle{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \omega

\displaystyle{\penalty\displaystyle\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;p(\omega)^{*}\alpha_{3}(\omega)\equiv p(\omega)^{*}\alpha_{3}(\omega)

which means (1. of Remark
5.1.2
) that the morphisms
f(\alpha_{1}(\omega)),\;p(\omega)^{*}\alpha_{3}(\omega)
and
\alpha_{2}(\omega),\alpha_{3}(\omega)
represent the same arrow of
\mathcal{C}
.

Now, let
\underline{\chi}^{\prime}
be a morphism
[\omega]\to[\delta^{\prime},x^{\prime}]
such that
[\delta^{\prime}]\underline{\chi}^{\prime}=\chi
and
[f(\delta^{\prime}),x^{\prime}]\underline{\chi}^{\prime}=\chi^{\prime}
. By 3. of Remark
5.1.2
, the morphism
\underline{\chi}^{\prime}
is represented by a morphism of contexts of the form:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \omega\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\beta_{1}(\omega):\Delta^{\prime},\;\beta_{2}(\omega):A(\textnormal{{c}}(f(\beta_{1}(\omega)))).

Moreover:

\displaystyle{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \omega

\displaystyle{\penalty\displaystyle\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;p_{1}(\omega):\alpha_{1}(\omega)=\beta_{1}(\omega)

\displaystyle{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \omega

\displaystyle{\penalty\displaystyle\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;p_{2}(\omega):\alpha_{2}(\omega)=f(\beta_{1}(\omega))

\displaystyle{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \omega

\displaystyle{\penalty\displaystyle\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;p_{3}(\omega):\alpha_{3}(\omega)=p_{2}(\omega)^{*}\beta_{2}(\omega)

by 1. and 4. or Remark
5.1.2
, hence
p_{1}(\omega):\alpha_{1}(\omega)=\beta_{1}(\omega)
and:

\displaystyle p(\omega)^{*}\alpha_{3}(\omega)

\displaystyle=p(\omega)^{*}p_{2}(\omega)^{*}\beta_{2}(\omega)

\displaystyle=(p(\omega)\bullet p_{2}(\omega))^{*}\beta_{2}(\omega)

\displaystyle=f(p_{1}(\omega))^{*}\beta_{2}(\omega)

\displaystyle=p_{1}(\omega)^{*}\beta_{2}(\omega)

where the first and the second equalities are instances of propositional functorialities, the fourth holds by multiple (generalised) path induction on
p_{1}(\omega)
and the third because
\gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime}
is an h-elementary context and by Corollary
5.0.3
. Therefore, the judgements
\alpha_{1}(\omega),\;p(\omega)^{*}\alpha_{3}(\omega)
and
\beta_{1}(\omega),\beta_{2}(\omega)
represent the same arrow
[\omega]\to[\delta^{\prime},x^{\prime}]
i.e.
\underline{\chi}=\underline{\chi}^{\prime}
.∎

Remark 5.4.2
.

The last part of the proof of the cartesianity of the natural transformation
P
is the crucial point where we needed to work with h-elementary contexts. Any other result that we obtained so far is true for the (generally larger) family of contexts with h-propositional identities.

We summarise the content of the current section into the following:

Theorem 5.4.3
.

The category
\mathcal{C}
of Proposition
5.1.1
, together with the presheaf
tp
of Proposition
5.2.1
, the semantic context extension
-.-
of Proposition
5.3.1
and the natural transformation
P
of Proposition
5.4.1
, forms a category with attributes
\bm{\mathfrak{C}}:=(\mathcal{C},\textsc{tp},-.-,P)
.

We end the current section with the following:

Remark 5.4.4
.

A terminal object preserving functor from the base category of
\bm{\mathfrak{C}}
to the base category of
\mathbf{eTT}
mapping:

(\;[\delta:\Delta]\xrightarrow{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\delta):\Gamma]}[\gamma:\Gamma]\;)\mapsto(\;|\delta|\xrightarrow{|{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\delta):\Gamma|}|\gamma|\;)=(\;|\delta|\xrightarrow{{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ |\delta|\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}_{\textnormal{{ext}}}\;\;\;|f(\delta)|:|\Gamma|}|\gamma|\;)

is well-defined by Proposition
5.0.6
and extends to a morphism of semantic types
\bm{\mathfrak{C}}\to\mathbf{eTT}
if we stipulate that:

[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}]\;\mapsto\;|{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}|\;=\;{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ |\gamma|\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}_{\textnormal{{ext}}}\;\;\;|A(\gamma)|:\textsc{Type}

(see Remark
2.3.13
for more details). Observe in fact that this operation is well-defined by Lemma
5.0.5
and is natural since
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ |\gamma|\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}_{\textnormal{{ext}}}\;\;\;|A(f(\delta))|\equiv|A|(|f(\delta)|)
by Remark
2.3.13
(here we are implicitly using that:

(\textsc{tp}[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\delta):\Gamma])[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}]=[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(f(\delta)):\textsc{Type}]

and this presentation of the substitution in
\bm{\mathfrak{C}}
is justified by Proposition
4.1.2
, Corollary
4.1.10
and Lemma
4.1.12
). One can verify that the semantic context extension and the display map family are preserved.

Section

6
is devoted to proving that
\bm{\mathfrak{C}}
, together with the structure specified in Theorem
5.4.3
, is a model of
ETT
.

6
A model of Extensional Type Theory

In this section, we show that
\bm{\mathfrak{C}}
verifies the requirements of Definition
2.3.7
where
T
is
ETT
. We start by observing that, if
T
is an atomic type of
ETT
and
t:T
is an atomic term of
ETT
, then
T
is an atomic h-set of
PTT
, hence the class
[\;{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;T:\textsc{Type}\;]
is a semantic type of
\bm{\mathfrak{C}}
in semantic context
[\;\_:\_\;]
and
[\;{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;t:T\;]
is a section of the corresponding display map, hence a semantic term of
[\;{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;T:\textsc{Type}\;]
in
\bm{\mathfrak{C}}
. Therefore the mappings:

\displaystyle T

\displaystyle\mapsto[\;{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;T:\textsc{Type}\;]

\displaystyle t

\displaystyle\mapsto[\;{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;t:T\;]

define a choice function as the one required in Definition
2.3.7
.

After studying an opportune presentation of the semantic terms of
\bm{\mathfrak{C}}
in
Section

6.1
, we show that
\bm{\mathfrak{C}}
has semantic extensional identity types, semantic dependent product types, and semantic dependent sum types. We present the proof for the extensional identities in full form, while we only leave a sketch of the corresponding ones for dependent products and sums. Finally, we deduce the conservativity result in
Section

6.4
.

6.1
A presentation of the sections in the quotient syntax

Let
[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}]
be some semantic type in some semantic context
[\gamma:\Gamma]
(by 1. of Remark
5.2.2
we do not lose generality if we assume this presentation for
[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}]
). Then, its component of
P
admits the presentation
[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma]
. Let us consider a section:

[\gamma]\to[\gamma].[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}]=[\gamma,x]

of
[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma]
, that, by 3. of Remark
5.1.2
, admits the presentation
[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\tilde{a}(\gamma):\Gamma,a(\gamma):A(\tilde{a}(\gamma))]
. Then
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\alpha(\gamma):\gamma=\tilde{a}(\gamma)
, by 1. of Remark
5.1.2
and being
[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\tilde{a}(\gamma),a(\gamma)]
a section of
[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma]
. Hence the morphism of contexts:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma,\alpha(\gamma)^{*}a(\gamma):A(\gamma)

is context propositionally equal to
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\tilde{a}(\gamma),a(\gamma)
and therefore it continues representing the given section
[\gamma]\to[\gamma,x]
.

We conclude that:

Remark 6.1.1
.

Without loss of generality, every section of a display map
[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma]
is of the form:

[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma,a(\gamma)]

for some term
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;a(\gamma):A(\gamma)
.

6.2
Semantic extensional identity types

Let
[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}]
be a semantic type in semantic context
[\gamma:\Gamma]
(see 1. or Remark
5.2.2
). A presentation of
[\gamma:\Gamma].[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}]
is
[\gamma,x:A(\gamma)]
, hence:

(\textsc{tp}[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma])([{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}])=[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\textnormal{{c}}(\gamma)):\textsc{Type}]

for some canonical homotopy equivalence
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
between
\gamma
and itself. In particular:

(\textsc{tp}[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma])([{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}])=[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}]

by Propostion
4.1.2
and by Corollary
4.1.10
. Therefore we obtain the presentation:

[\gamma,x:A(\gamma),y:A(\gamma)]

of
([\gamma:\Gamma].[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}]).((\textsc{tp}[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma])([{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}]))
. We define the semantic type
\textnormal{{id}}_{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}]}
in context
[\gamma,x,y]
as the one represented by the type judgement:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x,y\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;x=y:\textsc{Type}

and the morphism of contexts
\textnormal{{r}}_{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}]}
between
[\gamma,x]
and
[\gamma,x,y].[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x,y\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;x=y:\textsc{Type}]=[\gamma,x,y,p:x=y]
as the one represented by the context morphism:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma,x,x,\textnormal{{r}}(x).

We verified that
formation
and
introduction
of Definition
2.3.1
are satisfied. We are left to verify that:

Proposition 6.2.1
.

The remaining conditions of Definition
2.3.1
- i.e.
extensionality
and
compatibility with the substitution
- are satisfied by the above choice of:

\textnormal{{{id}}}_{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}]}\text{ and }\textnormal{{r}}_{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}]}

hence
\bm{\mathfrak{C}}
is equipped with semantic extensional identity types.

Proof.

\succ

Extensionality
. Let us consider two semantic terms:

[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma:\Gamma,a(\gamma):A(\gamma)]\textnormal{ and }[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma:\Gamma,b(\gamma):A(\gamma)]

of the semantic type
[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}]
in the semantic context
[\gamma:\Gamma]
. They admit such a presentation because of 3. of Remark
5.1.2
and Remark
6.1.1
. Now, if we consider
[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma:\Gamma,a(\gamma):A(\gamma)]
as an arrow of
\int\textsc{tp}
of source
([\gamma],[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}])
and target
([\gamma,x],[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}])
, its semantic context extension:

[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma:\Gamma,a(\gamma):A(\gamma)].[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}]

is:

[\gamma,x]\xrightarrow{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma,a(\gamma),x]}[\gamma,x,y]

hence the arrow:
[\gamma]\xrightarrow{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma,b(\gamma):A(\gamma)]}[\gamma,x]\xrightarrow{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma:\Gamma,a(\gamma):A(\gamma)].[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}]}[\gamma,x,y]
of Lemma
2.1.3
happens to be represented by
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma,a(\gamma),b(\gamma)
, because of 4. of Remark
5.1.2
.

By Proposition
4.1.2
, by Corollary
4.1.10
and by Lemma
4.1.12
, the semantic type:

\textsc{tp}[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma,a(\gamma),b(\gamma)](\textnormal{{id}}_{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}]})=\textsc{tp}[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma,a(\gamma),b(\gamma)]([{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x,y\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;x=y:\textsc{Type}])

in semantic context
[\gamma]
admits the presentation:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;a(\gamma)=b(\gamma):\textsc{Type}.

With this presentation, if we are given a semantic term of this semantic type in context
[\gamma]
, i.e. a section
[\gamma]\to[\gamma,p^{\prime}:a(\gamma)=b(\gamma)]
of the corresponding display map
[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,p^{\prime}:a(\gamma)=b(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma]
, then by Remark
6.1.1
it admits a representative of the form
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma:\Gamma,p(\gamma):a(\gamma)=b(\gamma)
for some judgement
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;p(\gamma):a(\gamma)=b(\gamma)
. Hence
[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma,a(\gamma)]=[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma,b(\gamma)]
, by 1. of Remark
5.1.2
. We are left to verify that:

[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma:\Gamma,p(\gamma):a(\gamma)=b(\gamma)]=\textnormal{{r}}_{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}]}^{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma:\Gamma,a(\gamma):A(\gamma)]}.

As the diagram (where
p^{\prime\prime}:a(\gamma)=a(\gamma)
):

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\gamma,x}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\gamma,p^{\prime\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\gamma,x,y,p}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma,a(\gamma)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma,\textnormal{{r}}(a(\gamma))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma,x,x,\textnormal{{r}}(x)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,p^{\prime\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma,a(\gamma),a(\gamma),p^{\prime\prime}}

commutes even judgementally and
{{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,p^{\prime\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma,a(\gamma),a(\gamma),p^{\prime\prime}}
represents the extension:

(\;[\gamma]\xrightarrow{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma,a(\gamma):A(\gamma)]}[\gamma,x]\xrightarrow{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma:\Gamma,a(\gamma):A(\gamma)].[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}]}[\gamma,x,y]\;).\textnormal{{id}}_{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}]}

then
\textnormal{{r}}_{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}]}^{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma:\Gamma,a(\gamma):A(\gamma)]}
is represented by
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma,\textnormal{{r}}(a(\gamma))
and we are left to verify that:

[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma,p(\gamma):a(\gamma)=b(\gamma)]=[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma,\textnormal{{r}}(a(\gamma))].

Let us consider a canonical context homotopy equivalence
\langle\;\varphi\;|\;\psi\;\rangle
between
\gamma
and itself such that
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\alpha(\gamma):\textnormal{{c}}(\gamma)=\gamma
and let
\langle\;\varphi\;|\;\psi\;\rangle
be a canonical homotopy equivalence between
A(\gamma)
and itself relative to
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
and such that
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\alpha_{1}(\gamma,x):\varphi(\gamma,x)=\alpha(\gamma)^{*}x
(see Proposition
4.1.2
and Lemma
4.1.3
). Now, if:

\displaystyle{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma

\displaystyle{\penalty\displaystyle\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{r}}_{1}(\gamma)\equiv\alpha_{1}(\gamma,a(\gamma))\bullet a(\alpha(\gamma))^{-1}:\varphi(\gamma,a(\gamma))=a(\textnormal{{c}}(\gamma))

\displaystyle{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma

\displaystyle{\penalty\displaystyle\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{r}}_{2}(\gamma)\equiv\alpha_{1}(\gamma,a(\gamma))\bullet a(\alpha(\gamma))^{-1}\bullet p(\textnormal{{c}}(\gamma)):\varphi(\gamma,a(\gamma))=b(\textnormal{{c}}(\gamma))

then the corresponding
\langle\;\varphi^{=}\;|\;\psi^{=}\;\rangle
of Lemma
3.4.1
happens to be a canonical homotopy equivalence between
a(\gamma)=a(\gamma)
and
a(\gamma)=b(\gamma)
relative to
\langle\;\textnormal{{c}}\;|\;\textnormal{{d}}\;\rangle
(see the notion of canonical homotopy equivalence in
Section

3.6
, as well as
Remark

3.6.1
) and by propositional groupoidality:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\varphi^{=}(\gamma,\textnormal{{r}}(a(\gamma)))=p(\textnormal{{c}}(\gamma)).

Therefore, the diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\gamma,p^{\prime\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\gamma,p^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma,\textnormal{{r}}(a(\gamma))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma,p(\gamma)}
c
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{{c}},\varphi^{=}}

commutes propositionally and, by the notion of canonical context homotopy equivalence, the morphism of contexts
\textnormal{{c}},\varphi^{=}
is the first component of a canonical context homotopy equivalence
\gamma,p^{\prime\prime}\to\gamma,p^{\prime}
(see Lemma
3.1.1
). Therefore
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma,\textnormal{{r}}(a(\gamma))
and
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma,p(\gamma)
represent the same morphism of semantic contexts i.e. the same arrow of
\mathcal{C}
and we are done.

\succ

Compatibility with the substitution
. If we are given a morphism of contexts:

[\delta:\Delta]\xrightarrow{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\delta):\Gamma]}[\gamma:\Gamma]

(such a representative exists by 3. of Remark
5.1.2
) then the semantic context extension
[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\delta):\Gamma].[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}]
admits the presentation:

[\delta,x^{\prime}:A(f(\delta))]\xrightarrow{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta,x^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\delta),x^{\prime}]}[\gamma,x:A(\gamma)]

and the further semantic context extension:

([{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\delta):\Gamma].[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}]).[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}]

admits the presentation:

[\delta,x^{\prime},y^{\prime}:A(f(\delta))]\xrightarrow{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta,x^{\prime},y^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\delta),x^{\prime},y^{\prime}]}[\gamma,x,y:A(\gamma)].

Hence, by Proposition
4.1.2
, by Corollary
4.1.10
and by Lemma
4.1.12
, the type:

\textsc{tp}(([{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\delta):\Gamma].[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}]).[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}])\textnormal{{id}}_{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}]}

in context
[\delta,x^{\prime},y^{\prime}]
is represented by
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta,x^{\prime},y^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;x^{\prime}=y^{\prime}
, which is a representative of
\textnormal{{id}}_{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(f(\delta)):\textsc{Type}]}
, and
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(f(\delta)):\textsc{Type}
represents the type:

(\textsc{tp}[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\delta):\Gamma])[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}]

in context
\delta
, again by Proposition
4.1.2
, by Corollary
4.1.10
and by Lemma
4.1.12
. We conclude that:

\textsc{tp}(([{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\delta):\Gamma].[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}]).[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}])\textnormal{{id}}_{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}]}=

=\textnormal{{id}}_{(\textsc{tp}[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\delta):\Gamma])[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}]}.

Now, the semantic context extension:

(([{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\delta):\Gamma].[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}]).[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}]).\textnormal{{id}}_{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}]}

admits the presentation:

[\delta,x^{\prime},y^{\prime},p^{\prime}:x^{\prime}=y^{\prime}]\xrightarrow{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta,x^{\prime},y^{\prime},p^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;f(\delta),x^{\prime},y^{\prime},p^{\prime}]}[\gamma,x,y,p:x=y]

hence we are left to verify that:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\delta,x^{\prime}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\gamma,x]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\delta,x^{\prime},y^{\prime},p^{\prime}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\gamma,x,y,p]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{{r}}_{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \delta\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(f(\delta))]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{{r}}_{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma)]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[f(\delta),x^{\prime}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[f(\delta),x^{\prime},y^{\prime},p^{\prime}]}

commutes in
\mathcal{C}
. By 1. and 4. of Remark
5.1.2
, we are done since the diagram of the representatives:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\delta,x^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\gamma,x}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\delta,x^{\prime},y^{\prime},p^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\gamma,x,y,p}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta,x^{\prime},x^{\prime},\textnormal{{r}}(x^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma,x,x,\textnormal{{r}}(x)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f(\delta),x^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f(\delta),x^{\prime},y^{\prime},p^{\prime}}

commutes even judgmentally.∎

6.3
Semantic dependent product and sum types

Let
[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}]
be a semantic type in semantic context
[\gamma:\Gamma]
and let:

[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;B(\gamma,x):\textsc{Type}]

be a semantic type in semantic context
[\gamma].[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}]=[\gamma,x]
, where, as usual, we refer to see 1. of Remark
5.2.2
for the presentation we use for the semantic types. Below, we define the choices of the semantic types and the semantic terms that we need so that
\bm{\mathfrak{C}}
is endowed with semantic dependent products and semantic dependent sums respectively.

\mathbf{{\Pi}}

Formation
. We define the semantic type
\mathbf{{\Pi}}_{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}]}^{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;B(\gamma,x):\textsc{Type}]}
in context
[\gamma]
as the semantic type of
\bm{\mathfrak{C}}
represented by the type judgement
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\Pi_{x:A(\gamma)}B(\gamma,x):\textsc{Type}
.

Introduction
. If we are given a semantic term:

[\gamma,x]\xrightarrow{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma,x,b(\gamma,x)]}[\gamma,x,y:B(\gamma,x)]

(see Remark
6.1.1
to justify this presentation of the semantic terms) of semantic type:

[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;B(\gamma,x):\textsc{Type}]

we define
\lambda[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma,x,b(\gamma,x)]
to be the semantic term
[\gamma]\to[\gamma,z:\Pi_{x:A(\gamma)}B(\gamma,x)]
of sematic type
[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\Pi_{x:A(\gamma)}B(\gamma,x):\textsc{Type}]
represented by the morphism of contexts:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma,\lambda x.b(x):\Pi_{x:A(\gamma)}B(\gamma,x)

of
PTT
.

Elimination
. If we are given semantic terms:

[\gamma]\xrightarrow{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma,z(\gamma)]}[\gamma,z]\text{ \, and \, }[\gamma]\xrightarrow{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma,a(\gamma)]}[\gamma,x]

of semantic type
[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\Pi_{x:A(\gamma)}B(\gamma,x):\textsc{Type}]
and
[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}]
respectively, then we define the semantic term:

[\gamma]\xrightarrow{\textnormal{{ev}}_{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma,z(\gamma)]}^{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma,a(\gamma)]}}[\gamma,y^{\prime}:B(\gamma,a(\gamma))]

of semantic type:

(\textsc{tp}[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma,a(\gamma)])[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;B(\gamma,x):\textsc{Type}]=[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;B(\gamma,a(\gamma))]

as the one represented by the morphism of contexts:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma,\textnormal{{ev}}(z(\gamma),a(\gamma)):B(\gamma,a(\gamma))

of
PTT
(the presentation we use for
(\textsc{tp}[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma,a(\gamma)])[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x:A(\gamma)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;B(\gamma,x):\textsc{Type}]
is justified as usual by Proposition
4.1.2
, by Corollary
4.1.10
and by Lemma
4.1.12
).

One can prove that:

Proposition 6.3.1
.

With the above choices the properties of
compatibility with the substitution
,
\beta
-reduction
, and
\eta
-expansion
of Definition
2.3.3
are satisfied, hence
\bm{\mathfrak{C}}
is equipped with semantic dependent product types.

\mathbf{{\Sigma}}

Formation
. We define the semantic type
\mathbf{{\Sigma}}_{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}]}^{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;B(\gamma,x):\textsc{Type}]}
in context
[\gamma]
as the semantic type of
\bm{\mathfrak{C}}
represented by the type judgement
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\Sigma_{x:A(\gamma)}B(\gamma,x):\textsc{Type}
.

Introduction
. We define the morphism
p_{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}]}^{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;B(\gamma,x):\textsc{Type}]}
making the diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\gamma,x,y]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\gamma,u]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\gamma,x]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\gamma]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{\mathbf{{\Sigma}}_{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}]}^{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;B(\gamma,x):\textsc{Type}]}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;B(\gamma,x):\textsc{Type}]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A(\gamma):\textsc{Type}]}^{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;B(\gamma,x):\textsc{Type}]}}

commute (where
u:\Sigma_{x:A(\gamma)}B(\gamma,x):\textsc{Type}
) as the one represented by the morphism of contexts:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x,y\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma,\langle x,y\rangle

of
PTT
.

Elimination
. If we are given a semantic type
[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,u\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;C(\gamma,u):\textsc{Type}]
in semantic context
[\gamma,u]
and a semantic term
[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x,y\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma,x,y,c(\gamma,x,y):C(\gamma,\langle x,y\rangle)]
of semantic type:

(\textsc{tp}[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x,y\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma,\langle x,y\rangle])[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,u\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;C(\gamma,u):\textsc{Type}]=[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x,y\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;C(\gamma,\langle x,y\rangle)]

- as usual, use 1. of Remark
5.2.2
to justify the presentation we use for the semantic types; use Remark
6.1.1
for the one of the semantic terms; use Proposition
4.1.2
, Corollary
4.1.10
and Lemma
4.1.12
for the one of the semantic type
(\textsc{tp}[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x,y\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma,\langle x,y\rangle])[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,u\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;C(\gamma,u):\textsc{Type}]
- then we define the semantic term:

[\gamma,u]\xrightarrow{\textnormal{{split}}_{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,x,y\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma,x,y,c(\gamma,x,y):C(\gamma,\langle x,y\rangle)]}}[\gamma,u,c]

of semantic type
[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,u\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;C(\gamma,u):\textsc{Type}]
as the one represented by the morphism of contexts:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma,u\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma,u,\textnormal{{split}}(c,\gamma,u):C(\gamma,u)

of
PTT
.

Again, one can prove that:

Proposition 6.3.2
.

With the above choices the properties of
computation
and
compatibility with the substitution
of Definition
2.3.5
are satisfied, hence
\bm{\mathfrak{C}}
is equipped with semantic dependent sum types.

We might summarise the results of the current section into the following:

Theorem 6.3.3
.

The category with attributes
\bm{\mathfrak{C}}
is a model of
ETT
.

6.4
Conservativity result

We remind that
hPTT
indicates the sub-theory of
PTT
generated by the atomic h-sets of
PTT
. In other words,
hPTT
is the propositional type theory whose contexts are the h-elementary contexts of
PTT
, whose type judgements are the h-elementary type judgements of
PTT
and whose term judgements are the term judgements of
PTT
of an h-elementary type in h-elementary context. We refer to
Section

2.3
for further details.

We proved that the category with attributes
\bm{\mathfrak{C}}
is a model of
ETT
(Theorem
6.3.3
). By Theorem
2.3.12
and Remark
2.3.10
there are:

\succ

a unique morphism
\{\cdot\}:\mathbf{eTT}\to\bm{\mathfrak{C}}
of
ETT
;

\succ

a unique morphism
\{\cdot\}^{\prime}:\textnormal{{h}}\mathbf{pTT}\to\bm{\mathfrak{C}}
of
hPTT
.

The diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{{h}}\mathbf{pTT}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\mathfrak{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{eTT}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{\cdot\}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{\cdot\}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces|\cdot|}

where
|\cdot|
is the canonical interpretation of Remark
2.3.13
, commutes by Remark
2.3.10
and by Theorem
2.3.12
. However, the quotient mapping
[\cdot]
defined by the equivalence relation of
Section

5
happens to be a morphism
\textnormal{{h}}\mathbf{pTT}\to\bm{\mathfrak{C}}
of
hPTT
. Since
\{\cdot\}^{\prime}=[\cdot]
by Theorem
2.3.12
, the diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textnormal{{h}}\mathbf{pTT}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\mathfrak{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{eTT}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[\cdot]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{\cdot\}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces|\cdot|}

commutes. Moreover, one can verify that the morphism of semantic types
\bm{\mathfrak{C}}\to\mathbf{eTT}
of Remark
5.4.4
is a morphism of
\mathbf{eTT}
. By Theorem
2.3.12
it must be a retraction of
\{\cdot\}
, hence
\{\cdot\}
is injective on semantic contexts, morphisms between them, and semantic types.

Now, let
\gamma:\Gamma
be an h-elementary context of
PTT
and let
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;T(\gamma):\textsc{Type}
be an h-elementary judgement of
PTT
. Let us suppose that:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ |\gamma:\Gamma|\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}_{\textnormal{{ext}}}\;\;\;t(|\gamma:\Gamma|):|T(\gamma)|

in
ETT
. Here we recall that
|T(\gamma)|
denotes the type of
ETT
in context
|\gamma|
constituting the type judgement
|{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;T(\gamma):\textsc{Type}|
in
ETT
(remind that
|\cdot|
needs to map semantic types in semantic context
\gamma
- i.e. h-elementary type judgements of
PTT
in context
\gamma
- to semantic types in semantic context
|\gamma|
- i.e. type judgements of
ETT
in context
|\gamma|
-), see Remark
2.3.13
for more details. Hence, in
\mathbf{eTT}
the judgement
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ |\gamma|\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}_{\textnormal{{ext}}}\;\;\;|\gamma|,t(|\gamma|):|T(\gamma)|
is a morphism of semantic contexts:

|\gamma|\to|\gamma|.|{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;T(\gamma):\textsc{Type}|

happening to be a section of the display map
|\gamma|.|{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;T(\gamma):\textsc{Type}|\xrightarrow{P_{|{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;T(\gamma):\textsc{Type}|}}|\gamma|
. Therefore, being
\{\cdot\}
a morphism of
ETT
, we get a section:

\displaystyle[\gamma]=\{|\gamma|\}\xrightarrow{\{{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ |\gamma|\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}_{\textnormal{{ext}}}\;\;\;|\gamma|,t(|\gamma|):|T(\gamma)|\}}\{|\gamma|.|{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;T(\gamma):\textsc{Type}|\}

\displaystyle=\{|\gamma|\}.\{|{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;T(\gamma):\textsc{Type}|\}

\displaystyle=[\gamma].[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;T(\gamma):\textsc{Type}]

\displaystyle=[\gamma:\Gamma,t:T(\gamma)]

of:

[\gamma:\Gamma,t:T(\gamma)]\xrightarrow{\;\;P_{\{|{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;T(\gamma):\textsc{Type}|\}}=P_{[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;T(\gamma):\textsc{Type}]}\;\;}[\gamma]

in
\bm{\mathfrak{C}}
, where the last equality follows by Proposition
4.1.2
and by Corollary
4.1.10
. By Remark
6.1.1
, the arrow
\{{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ |\gamma:\Gamma|\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}_{\textnormal{{ext}}}\;\;\;|\gamma|,t(|\gamma|):|T(\gamma)|\}
happens to be represented by a morphism of contexts:

{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma:\Gamma,\tilde{t}(\gamma):T(\gamma)

for some judgement
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\tilde{t}(\gamma):T(\gamma)
. In particular:

\displaystyle\{{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ |\gamma|\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}_{\textnormal{{ext}}}\;\;\;|\gamma|,|\tilde{t}(\gamma)|:|T(\gamma)|\}

\displaystyle=\{|{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma,\;\tilde{t}(\gamma):T(\gamma)|\}

\displaystyle=[{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\gamma,\;\tilde{t}(\gamma):T(\gamma)]

\displaystyle=\{{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ |\gamma|\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}_{\textnormal{{ext}}}\;\;\;|\gamma|,t(|\gamma|):|T(\gamma)|\}

and, by injectivity of
\{\cdot\}
on morphisms of semantic contexts, we conclude that
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ |\gamma|\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}_{\textnormal{{ext}}}\;\;\;|\tilde{t}(\gamma)|\equiv t(|\gamma|)
. Hence we have just proven the following:

Theorem 6.4.1

(Conservativity)
.

Let
\gamma:\Gamma
be an h-elementary context of
PTT
and let
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;T(\gamma:\Gamma):\textsc{Type}
be an h-elementary type judgement of
PTT
. Whenever
ETT
infers
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ |\gamma:\Gamma|\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}_{\textnormal{{ext}}}\;\;\;t(|\gamma:\Gamma|):|T(\gamma)|
, then
PTT
infers
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ \gamma:\Gamma\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\tilde{t}(\gamma:\Gamma):T(\gamma:\Gamma)
and
ETT
infers
{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ |\gamma:\Gamma|\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}_{\textnormal{{ext}}}\;\;\;|\tilde{t}(\gamma:\Gamma)|\equiv t(|\gamma:\Gamma|)
.

We infer the following:

Theorem 6.4.2
.

If the atomic types of
PTT
are all h-sets, then
ETT
is conservative over
PTT
. In particular, if the only atomic types of
PTT
are the empty type, the unit type, the type of booleans and the natural numbers type, then
ETT
is conservative over
PTT
.

7
Inference rules for dependent type theories

In this section we enumerate the inference rules that we consider in this paper, particularly for what we call
extensional
,
intensional
, and
propositional
type theories with three type constructors: identities, dependent products and dependent sums. We omit the usual structural rules of a strict dependent type theory - that every theory considered in this paper is assumed to satisfy. For an enumeration of the structural rules, see
[
Str91b
, Chapter III - context formation, context equality, judgement formation, judgement equality]
,
[
Jac99
]
,
[
Hof97
]
.

7.1
Identity types

\displaystyle\text{Formation \quad}

\displaystyle\frac{\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0.54,0.66,0.36}\gamma:\Gamma}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A{\color[rgb]{0.54,0.66,0.36}(\gamma)}:\textsc{Type}\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad}{{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0.54,0.66,0.36}\gamma:\Gamma;\;}x,x^{\prime}:A{\color[rgb]{0.54,0.66,0.36}(\gamma)}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;x=x^{\prime}:\textsc{Type}}

\displaystyle\text{Introduction \quad}

\displaystyle\frac{\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0.54,0.66,0.36}\gamma:\Gamma}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A{\color[rgb]{0.54,0.66,0.36}(\gamma)}:\textsc{Type}\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad}{{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0.54,0.66,0.36}\gamma:\Gamma;\;}x:A{\color[rgb]{0.54,0.66,0.36}(\gamma)}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\textnormal{{r}}(x):x=x}

\displaystyle\text{Extensionality \quad}

\displaystyle\frac{\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0.54,0.66,0.36}\gamma:\Gamma}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A{\color[rgb]{0.54,0.66,0.36}(\gamma)}:\textsc{Type}\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad}{{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0.54,0.66,0.36}\gamma:\Gamma;\;}x,x^{\prime}:A{\color[rgb]{0.54,0.66,0.36}(\gamma)};\;p:x=x^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;x\equiv x^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\frac{\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0.54,0.66,0.36}\gamma:\Gamma}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A{\color[rgb]{0.54,0.66,0.36}(\gamma)}:\textsc{Type}\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad}{{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0.54,0.66,0.36}\gamma:\Gamma;\;}x,x^{\prime}:A{\color[rgb]{0.54,0.66,0.36}(\gamma)};\;p:x=x^{\prime}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;p\equiv\textnormal{{r}}(x)}

\displaystyle\text{Prop. $\Pi$-extensionality \quad}

\displaystyle\frac{{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0.54,0.66,0.36}\gamma:\Gamma}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;A{\color[rgb]{0.54,0.66,0.36}(\gamma)}:\textsc{Type}\quad{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0.54,0.66,0.36}\gamma:\Gamma;\;}x:A{\color[rgb]{0.54,0.66,0.36}(\gamma)}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;B({\color[rgb]{0.54,0.66,0.36}\gamma,\;}x):\textsc{Type}\quad\quad\quad\quad{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0.54,0.66,0.36}\gamma:\Gamma;\;}z,z^{\prime}:\Pi_{x:A{\color[rgb]{0.54,0.66,0.36}(\gamma)}}B({\color[rgb]{0.54,0.66,0.36}\gamma,\;}x);\;x:A{\color[rgb]{0.54,0.66,0.36}(\gamma)}\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\quad\quad{p({\color[rgb]{0.54,0.66,0.36}\gamma,\;}z,z^{\prime},x):\textnormal{{ev}}(z,x)=\textnormal{{ev}}(z^{\prime},x)}}{\text{ }\hfil{\\
}{\\
}{\bm{\lfloor}}\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0.54,0.66,0.36}\gamma:\Gamma;\;}z,z^{\prime}:\Pi_{x:A{\color[rgb]{0.54,0.66,0.36}(\gamma)}}B({\color[rgb]{0.54,0.66,0.36}\gamma,\;}x)\penalty\ {\bm{\rfloor}}\;\;\;\rho(p,{\color[rgb]{0.54,0.66,0.36}\gamma,\;}z,z^{\prime}):z=z^{\prime}}

Figure 5:
Propositional dependent product types

In the next section we recall a characterisations of dependent sum types and propositional sum types
inside

ETT
and
PTT
, respectively. In fact we recall that:

\succ

a dependent type theory with extensional identity types has the rules of
Figure

6
if and only if it has the rules of
Figure

8
;

\succ

a dependent type theory with propositional identity types has the rules of
Figure

7
if and only if it has the rules of
Figure

9
.

For further details, we refer the reader to
[
Jac99
,
See84
]
and to
[
Spa22
]
, respectively.
